| A delta is a diff | Applying a delta makes the automatic rows of a source equal to it: identical rows are not written (ids and timestamps stay), different ones are updated in place, absent ones are removed, manual rows are never touched. This is what makes a second pass over unchanged files change nothing (I14). A mention on a merged entity matches the delta's mention of the survivor, so a merge does not make the next pass rewrite it |
| Attribute provenance | One row per `(entity, key, value, source)` (a unique index with `COALESCE(source_id, 0)`), where the master schema had `(entity, key, value)`. Two files stating the same email each keep their row, so losing one file removes only its own statement; a manual row has no source |
| Merge is a redirect | `merge_entities(survivor, victim)` resolves both to what they currently stand for, refuses the same entity (which makes a cycle impossible), refuses two different types and a tombstone, flattens whatever pointed at the victim onto the survivor, moves nothing, and logs ids in `kb_ops_log` so `unmerge_entity` can give back the victim and what was redirected on its behalf. Unmerge is refused when a live entity has taken the name in the meantime |
| Delete is a tombstone | `delete_entity` removes the mentions and relations, keeps the name (so an analysis does not recreate it), the aliases and the attributes (so a restore loses nothing). A merged entity cannot be deleted: unmerge it, or delete the survivor. `restore_entity` restores the previous status. No file, chunk or inventory row is touched (I11) |
| What the collector keeps | Only automatic, live (`candidate` or `active`) entities with no mention, no relation, no manual alias, no manual attribute and nothing merged into them are collected. Manual entities, merged entities and tombstones record a decision and stay |
| Resets | Documents Reset and a vanished document clear the `documents` sources, Data Reset and a vanished workbook clear the `data` sources, each in the transaction of the event; the typed cell cache is not a knowledge event. `IndexStore::clear_knowledge(include_manual)` exists for the later dialog and is wired to no command. The commands `reset_index` and `reset_data_index` needed no change: the store methods they already call do it |
| Machine codes | `knowledge_unavailable`, `knowledge_entity_not_found`, `knowledge_merge_refused`, localised in both catalogues. A knowledge failure never replaces the index's own `index_unavailable` for an index failure |
| Nothing is visible yet | No command, no setting, no screen reads the knowledge base: lot 1 is the foundation. The tables fill in lot 4 (documents) and lot 5 (tables) |

## The knowledge base never blocks indexing (owner decision, 9 October 2026)

| Subject | Decision |
| --- | --- |
| The rule | Indexing and analysis keep working, even in a minimal way, whatever happens to the knowledge base. If a knowledge step fails (extraction, writing the knowledge delta, a locked or damaged `kb_*` table), the pass **bypasses the knowledge steps** and still indexes the document's chunks, so questions, retrieval and the Resets go on as before. The knowledge base is an addition to the product, never a condition of it |
| The user is told | A bypassed step is reported at the end of the pass, in the user's language, as a partial failure: part of the analysis did not complete and running the analysis again is advised. It is not an error that stops the pass, and it is not silent. The report carries a machine code and counts (how many files), never a name or a passage |
| Retrying is natural | A document whose knowledge step was bypassed is recorded with `kb_version = 0` or has no `kb_sources` row, so the next pass sees it as due (`should_skip` must take that into account in lot 4). No separate retry queue |
| Where it is built | Lot 4 (document ingestion), which owns the extraction and extends `IndexSummary` with the knowledge summary and its interface strings: a knowledge failure is applied in a savepoint inside the document's transaction, rolled back alone, and the chunks are committed. Lot 5 does the same for tables. Lot 6 already falls back to the full selection when a lookup fails (invariant I4) |
| Lot 1 as merged | The one case that still rolls the chunks back with the knowledge is a database error while applying the delta in `replace_document_with_knowledge`. With the empty delta lot 1 writes this is practically unreachable (a source upsert and a collection query). It is lot 4's first job to remove it, with a test that corrupts a `kb_*` table and checks that the document is still indexed and the pass reports the partial failure |
| Operator logs | An incident log a maintenance operator could read is wanted, to trace failures at a practice. Deliberately **not** designed now: recorded in `docs/ROADMAP.md`, "Later, with no date" |

## Settled by KB lot 2, names, accents and sounds (9 October 2026)

| Subject | Decision |
| --- | --- |
| One door for folding text | `knowledge::normalize::fold` is the body `file_reference::fold_text` always had, moved unchanged. `fold_text` keeps its path and delegates, so a dozen modules compile as before; a test compares the old body, kept verbatim in the test, with the new one over every file and folder name of the fixture directories plus decomposed (Mac) and composed (Windows) accents |
| Three normal forms | `fold` for free text and file names (exact comparison); `normalize_name` for names (tokens, titles, particles, initials, an order-free `token_set_key`, a `compact` form, a `without_elision` form); `normalize_identifier` for identifiers (letters and digits, upper case). A cedilla is the one accent whose sound survives: `sound_form` turns `c` + cedilla into `s` before the accent is dropped, and only the phonetic encoder reads it. Exact comparison never depends on it |
| Words are data | Titles, ambiguous titles, particles and file-name stop-words are a `TitleSet` the caller passes in; no Rust literal holds one. The master named only titles; the same object also carries the particles and stop-words because every function that needs one needs the others. Lot 3 builds it from the lexicon packs. Until then `tests/fixtures/knowledge/name-words-fr.json` stands in for a pack |
| "Me" and "M" | Ambiguous titles count as titles only at the start of a name and, in the default `Auto` mode, only when written with a capital and followed by a capitalised word ("Me Martin": a title; "me martin", "Jean M Dupont": not). The extractor, which sees the sentence, can force either answer with `WeakTitles::Always` or `Never` |
| What separates words | Any character that is not a letter, a digit or an accent, apostrophes included; the master said to "drop other punctuation", but dropping a comma would glue "Dupont,Jean" into one word. An apostrophe also marks the word before it as an elided article ("l" of "l'Hopital"): kept in `tokens`, left out of the key, of the initials and of the sound |
| A key is a hint | Two names with the same phonetic key are a possible match offered to the user, never a merge (master section 7). That is why the rules are generous. The key alphabet is plain ASCII, documented at the top of `phonetic.rs`: consonants upper case, vowels upper case, nasal vowels lower case (`a` for "an", `o` for "on", `e` for "in") |
| French rules worth knowing | A silent final consonant (`t d s p`, and `x` after `u`) is dropped, but never below two letters, so the first sound of a key is the first sound of the name. A mute final `e` is dropped and remembered: it keeps the `n` of "Martine" and "Simone" pronounced (so they differ from "Martin" and "Simon") and the `s` of "Francoise" voiced. The `p` of "Philippe" is dropped on purpose so it meets "Filip", which the lot required. Final `er` and `ez` are pronounced like an acute `e`. "Francois" written without its cedilla, as every sanitised file name is, sounds like "Francois" with it |
| Homophones that are different people | "Durand" and "Durant" collide (they sound the same); so do "Dubois" and "Duboit" and "Henri" and "Henry". "Martin"/"Martine", "Simon"/"Simone", "Francois"/"Francoise", "Laurent"/"Laurence" and "Michel"/"Michelle" do not. The last pair sounds the same, but is a man and a woman, so on the owner's decision of 9 October 2026 a doubled "l" before a final mute "e" after "e" ("Michelle", "Danielle", "Gabrielle") ends its key with a sounded vowel (`MIXEL` against `MIXELE`); lot 2 bis generalises this with the sex of first names. The contract lives in `tests/fixtures/knowledge/phonetic-fr.json`, which the owner can extend without touching Rust (`cargo test --test knowledge_names -- --nocapture` prints the table of which names share a key) |
| Order is part of a name's key | `encode_name` encodes the words in the order written, so "Jean Dupont" and "Dupont Jean" have different phonetic keys. The order-free comparison is `token_set_key`; lot 3 stores the `reordered` alias with its own key |
| Encoders by locale | `FrenchPhonetic` (`fr-rules`, version 1; version 2 since lot 2 bis) and `EnglishPhonetic` (a conventional Soundex, `en-soundex`, version 1; replaced by `en-rules` version 1 in lot 2 bis) behind `PhoneticEncoder`; `encoder_for_locale` picks one and falls back to French like every other locale lookup. `signature()` is `id:version`, the string lot 3 stores in `kb_meta.phonetic_version` and compares at open to recompute stored keys |
| No new dependency | About 200 lines of rules, `unicode-normalization` already in use. No phonetic crate was evaluated: the rules are short, readable and the owner's to correct |

## Defects found during the Knowledge Base programme (owner instruction, 9 October 2026)

| Subject | Decision |
| --- | --- |
| Where they live | `docs/test-reports/knowledge-base-pass-1/defects-register.md`, versioned. Every defect found by an agent preparing a test, by a lot's tests or by the owner's replay is recorded there at once, with its evidence, whether or not the KB caused it |
| What is analysed | Each defect is checked against the lots still to come: does it get in the way of the KB, or will it? The answer, with the lot numbers and the reason, is part of the row |
| A blocker | Fixed first, in its own `fix/kb-<topic>` branch from `kb/integration`, before the lot it blocks. The agent stops and tells the owner |
| Anything else | Left alone during the programme (each lot stays reviewable) and marked `deferred`. Lot 11 proposes the **targeted fix battery** for all of them, and its report is not signed off while one has no proposed fix. The fixes are to be done after the KB, not forgotten |
| Who knows | The always-applied rule `.cursor/rules/kb-programme.mdc` (duty 5), the workflow (section 2.4), the master, the launchers of lots 3 to 11 and the status file |

## Names calibrated on real data (owner decision, 9 October 2026)

| Subject | Decision |
| --- | --- |
| Why | The French phonetic key and the English Soundex of KB lot 2 are checked only against a table written with them. Public lists of real names break that circle, and the speech work that follows the KB (repair of dictated names) depends on the same keys |
| Where | A lot inserted between lot 2 and lot 3, "lot 2 bis", branch `feat/kb-names-calibration`. It changes the encoders' rules and fixtures and adds a measuring harness; it changes no schema, no other lot's scope and no threshold of another lot |
| Sources | Insee "Fichier des noms" and "Fichier des prénoms" (Licence Ouverte 2.0), US Census 2010 surnames, US SSA baby names. Licences and what citing them requires: `docs/DATA-SOURCES.md`. Full files are never versioned; only small derived samples, with the citation in the README |
| French and English together | Same harness, same lot, instead of a second pass later. Reason: one tool, one review, and dictation in English is wanted for the speech work |
| Owner's time | Kept short: automatic measures first (empty or coarse keys, determinism), then the sex rule (a masculine and a feminine first name that are clearly gendered never share a key, a source of `differ` rows only), then one review of at most 100 pairs with a pre-filled proposal where she marks only the exceptions |
| Thresholds | The plan uses a minimum token length with "same key and edit distance <= 1" in three places that disagree: 6 characters (master section 7, signal 6), 4 (targeting fallback, lot 6), 4 (transcript repair, lot 9). The lot measures the number of suggestions per realistic selection at lengths 3 to 6 and recommends; the constants stay with lots 3, 6 and 9 |
| What it does not do | No second encoder for names of other origins, no pronunciation oracle, no clinical vocabulary (drug names are a separate decision, see SpeechLab D-036), no About page |

## Settled by KB lot 2 bis, names calibrated on real data (9 October 2026)

| Subject | Decision |
| --- | --- |
| What was measured | The two encoders on the complete Insee lists (218 982 surnames, 42 942 first names after folding accents) and on the US Census 2010 surnames (162 253) and SSA first names (105 966, every year summed). Method, tables and numbers: `docs/test-reports/knowledge-base-pass-1/lots/lot-02-bis-names-calibration.md`. The harness is `apps/desktop/src-tauri/tests/knowledge_names_calibration.rs`; the lists are downloaded by `scripts/fetch_name_lists.py` into `data/names/` (git-ignored) |
| English: Soundex is replaced | A conventional Soundex put 622 surnames under `B620` and 829 first names under `S500` ("John", "Jane", "Jean", "Juan" and "Joan" all `J500`), and 1 343 pairs of clearly masculine and feminine first names shared a key. The replacement is an own rule set, `en-rules` version 1, behind the same port, no dependency: vowels kept in a few classes, silent letters dropped, "ph", "sh", "ch", "th", "ck", "kn", "wr", magic "e", a feminine marker on "-elle" and "-ianne". Largest group 22 surnames and 60 first names (60 is a family of spellings: "Aaliyah", "Aliyah", "Alia"); 9 opposite-sex pairs left, all true homophones |
| French: `fr-rules` goes to version 2 | Five rules, each from a measured fault: a final doubled "s" is sounded ("Anass"); a final "s" after "a" is sounded unless the letter before is "m", "c" or "l" ("Elias" against "Thomas", "Lucas", "Nicolas"); a final "d" after "i" is sounded ("Khalid", "Hamid", "Said", but not "Schmidt", where a "t" went first); a final "m" after "i", "ia", "ya", "ha" is sounded ("Karim", "Mariam", "Ilham"); the feminine marker widens from "elle" to a mute "e" after a vowel and "l" or "ll" and to "-ique" ("Michele", "Paule", "Pascale", "Nicolle", "Frederique") so that these never share a key with the masculine form. Also a final "y" after "a", "e" or "o" reads as "i" ("Leroy", "Gay"), and "oel" keeps its two vowels ("Noel"). Pairs of opposite-sex first names sharing a key: 62 before, 19 after |
| No name has an empty key | 75 Insee surnames had one ("Maitre" read as a title, "Le" and "Le Du" as particles, "Le Floc'H" because every word before an apostrophe was treated as an elided article). `normalize_name` now treats as elided only a one-letter word ("l'", "d'") and falls back to the particles, then the titles, when nothing else is left to encode. Recorded in the defects register (KBD-10) |
| The sex rule | Two first names of opposite sex, each at least 90 % one sex in the Insee or SSA counts, never share a key. It produces `differ` rows only. What no rule can separate (a real homophone: "Billy" and "Billie", "Eddy" and "Heidi", "Gene" and "Jean", "Randy" and "Randi") is listed in `known_shared` of the contract file, with a test that fails when a listed pair no longer shares a key and when an unlisted pair does. Today: 19 French pairs, 9 English pairs. A `false_friends` list does the same for pairs the owner judged to be two different names that the key cannot separate (`Martin`/`Martins`, `David`/`Davide`, `Georges`/`Georget`, `Nicolas`/`Nicola`, `Chen`/`Shen`). The rule covers 1 134 429 French and 710 010 English opposite-sex pairs |
| Gate for "same key and edit distance <= 1" | Measured at minimum lengths 3 to 6. A selection of 30 names drawn by frequency from the 5 000 most frequent contains 0.04 pairs the gate would link, and one mention in 350 of a name that is not selected draws a suggestion, at every length from 3 to 5; 6 removes 40 to 50 % of the pairs (476 to 296 French surnames, 1 144 to 548 French first names among the top names) and the pairs it removes are mostly real variants ("Marc"/"Mark", "Henri"/"Henry", "Alain"/"Allain", "Cook"/"Cooke"). Recommendation: **4 for all three places** (master section 7 signal 6, lot 6 fallback, lot 9 repair), 3 never (a one-letter edit changes a third of the name). The owner's review (10 October 2026) agreed with every proposal: of 48 suggestions drawn from the most frequent names, 41 were right and the 7 wrong ones were suffixed or foreign forms of another name, not short typos, so a longer minimum does not help. The constants stay with lots 3, 6 and 9 |
| Names from elsewhere | No second encoder. Thirty frequent Arabic, Vietnamese, Portuguese, Turkish, Polish, Spanish and Italian surnames get a deterministic ASCII key that follows the French reading (nasal vowels, a silent "d" or "t" at the end, "Haddad" `ADA`). Usable as a hint next to the edit distance, not as a pronunciation |
| Lists and citation | The complete lists are never versioned. The repository holds the 300 most frequent names of each list with their counts (`tests/fixtures/knowledge/names-sample-*.tsv`), cited in `README.md`, "Sources". The first look at the Insee first-name file was wrong about its encoding: it is UTF-8 without a byte order mark, upper case with accents; Windows tools that assume another code page show it as damaged. The US SSA site refuses clients that do not look like a browser (HTTP 403 with `curl`); `scripts/fetch_name_lists.py` sends ordinary browser headers and falls back to a manual download |
| The owner's review | Run on 10 October 2026, 88 rows, **no exception**. Every row became a contract row, and six rules were fixed to match her answers (`Michelle`/`Michele`, `Diane`/`Dianne`, `Daniel`/`Daniele`, `Henri`/`Emrys`, `Mitchell`/`Michel`, `Tran`/`Trahan`). Versions stay `fr-rules:2` and `en-rules:1`: no key is stored yet |

## Settled by the gateway fix "thinking and keep-alive" (10 October 2026)

| Subject | Decision |
| --- | --- |
| Thinking switch | `LLM_THINK` in the gateway, `default` (nothing sent, today's behaviour) or `off` (`"think": false` at the top level of the Ollama payload). There is no `on`: a model without the capability refuses `think: true` with HTTP 400. The default stays `default` until the second pass of the small-model comparison shows that answers hold with thinking off |
| Hidden reasoning is a length, never a text | The provider reads `message.thinking` only to count its characters (`GenerationChunk.thinking_chars`, register field `thinking_chars`). The text is never read further, kept, returned or logged (invariant I8) |
| A generation with no visible text is an error | The gateway answers `empty_answer` (HTTP 502, or an error event in the stream) instead of a blank message; the desktop shows one plain sentence from its two catalogues. It applies to every chat call, including the query-plan call of the tabular interpreter, which already treated a failed call as "no plan". The register records `outcome "empty_answer"` |
| Keep-alive | `OLLAMA_KEEP_ALIVE` defaults to `30m` in `compose.yaml` (Ollama's own: 5 minutes, against loads of 36 to 210 s on the development PC). `-1` on the Mac mini keeps the model loaded. The owner's `.env` is never edited by an agent |

## Settled by KB lot 3, ports, lexicon packs and the resolver (10 October 2026)

| Subject | Decision |
| --- | --- |
| Regex engine | The `regex` crate (MIT / Apache-2.0), added as a direct dependency with `default-features = false` and the features `std`, `perf`, `unicode-perl` (`\d`, `\s`, `\b`) and `unicode-case`. It was already in the dependency tree (1.13.1, through `tauri-utils`), so the build gains no code and `Cargo.lock` gains one line. Matching is linear-time with no backtracking, so a pattern in a pack cannot cause a ReDoS. A pack's pattern is compiled with a size limit of 256 KiB, and a pattern that matches the empty string is refused. Pure Rust, nothing platform-specific |
| The pack format | JSON, `schema: 1`, one file per pack and locale under `resources/knowledge/base/` and `resources/knowledge/packs/<id>/`, embedded with `include_str!` like `resources/tabular-questions`. Fields as in the master (`titles`, `title_spoken`, `stop_words`, `org_markers`, `identifier_schemes`, `columns`, `relations`, `roles`, `detection_terms`) **plus two the master did not list and lot 2 asked for**: `weak_titles` (titles that are also ordinary words or initials, "Me" and "M") and `particles` ("de", "van"). `titles` is keyed by entity type. Unknown fields are refused (`deny_unknown_fields`), so a typo or a stray "message" field cannot pass. Every list entry is a word or a phrase of at most four words with no sentence punctuation; a role is shown through a `label_key` under `knowledge.` that the interface catalogues resolve (`knowledge.roles.*`, added in both languages in this lot) |
| A domain pack only adds | It has no field that could change an entity type. A header that two active packs map to different types is refused when the set is assembled; the same mapping twice is fine. An identifier scheme id is unique across the active packs. A role used by a column or a relation must be defined by some active pack. The last active pack that defines a role decides its label key, which is how "Client" becomes "Patient" in the health pack without changing what a client is. The base holds the seven neutral roles and nothing specific to a profession |
| Locale fallback and fingerprint | The exact tag, then any tag of the same language, then `fr-FR`, the order the tabular packs and the interface already use. `PackSet::hash()` (stored in `kb_meta.packs_hash` by `maintenance::record_packs`) is a SHA-256 over the canonical JSON of every loaded pack (keys sorted, no whitespace), so it changes with the content, the set of active packs and their order, and with nothing else: not indentation, not line endings, not key order |
| The starter packs | `base`, `health-fr`, `legal-fr`, `accounting-fr`, each in French and English (named `<domain>-<country>` since 11 October 2026, see "Knowledge packs are named domain-country"; they were `health`, `legal`, `accounting` when this lot shipped). They are starting vocabulary, not a finished lexicon: counts are printed by `cargo test --test knowledge_packs print_the_summary_of_every_pack -- --nocapture`. A pack never holds a sentence: `a_pack_holds_words_and_phrases_never_a_sentence` reads the raw files |
| **The minimum word length for "same sound and one edit" is 4** | A named constant, `resolve::FUZZY_MIN_TOKEN_LEN = 4`, **not the 6 of master section 7**. Lot 2 bis measured it on the complete Insee, US Census and SSA lists and the owner reviewed the result (10 October 2026); lots 6 and 9 use 4 as well. The owner can overrule by changing this one constant. The rule: the two names have the same number of words, exactly one word differs, by exactly one Damerau-Levenshtein edit, both versions of that word have at least four letters, and the phonetic keys are the same. It produces a `PossibleMatch`, never a link |
| Token-set equality is a hint | "The same words in another order" is signal 6 in the master, so it is only ever a `PossibleMatch` (score 0.75). For a two-word person the surname-first order is an exact alias (`reordered`) and resolves; the order-free comparison covers the longer names no alias anticipates |
| What an entity is born with | `resolve::aliases_for`: the canonical form, the form without an elided article ("l'Hopital Central" is also "Hopital Central"), and for a person with a given name and a surname the reordered form, the initial and the surname (`j dupont`), the surname alone, and a title and the surname for each title **seen** with that person. An organisation's acronym only when one was seen. The surname keeps the particles in front of it ("de fontaine" and "fontaine"). Each alias carries the phonetic key of the active encoder. A duplicate normalised form is dropped so the unique key of `kb_aliases` is never hit |
| Resolver behaviour (the table of master section 7) | Tombstone first (a deleted name resolves to nothing and creates nothing). In **query** mode every hit is first restricted to the selection, so ambiguity is judged inside the selection and an entity outside it is never named; a known name that is only outside the selection is `Unresolved(not_in_selection)`. A surname alone, or a title and a surname, is "this person" only when exactly one entity answers (confidence 0.80, the reduction threshold); two or more is `Ambiguous`. An initial and a surname is a `PossibleMatch` (0.60). In **ingestion** mode a surname or an initial links only to an entity **of the same source** and only when nothing else in the source answers to it (confidence 0.70, under the threshold), otherwise a surname stays `Unresolved(surname_only)` and an initial becomes a separate candidate; the caller passes the entities seen so far in the file, and without that list the resolver declines rather than guess. A candidate entity resolves in query mode with a confidence capped at 0.40, so a guess can rank but never narrow. A manual alias or entity beats an automatic one; two manual ones tie. Identifier signal: exact collision links (1.00), a "possible" scheme such as a phone number never does |
| Confidence by method | `Method` (`identifier` 1.00, `column_value` 0.95, `gazetteer_hit` 0.90, `title_full_name` 0.85, `organization_marker` 0.80, `title_surname` 0.70, `filename_token` 0.50, `capitalised_name` 0.40, `manual` 1.00), the master's initial values plus one for "title and full name". A new entity is `Active` only when its method is supported (everything except title and surname, file-name word, capitalised run) and, for a person, the name is not a single word unless a column or an identifier vouched for it. Tuned in lot 11, in one place |
| A lookup that cannot be read is not "nothing" | `EntityLookup::failed()`: the store-backed lookup records a failed read and the resolver answers `Unresolved(lookup_failed)`, so an ingestion pass never creates a duplicate because it could not look, and a question falls back to the full selection (invariant I4) |
| The resolver never writes | It reads through `EntityLookup` and returns a `Resolution`; creating the entity, the alias, the `kb_possible_matches` row and the mention is the caller's job (lot 4), in the transaction of the document. A test asserts that resolving does not change a row |
| The gazetteer | In memory, built once per pass from the aliases of active entities plus anything the user typed (a candidate the extractor guessed is left out, so a guess cannot vouch for the next one), phrases up to five words, leftmost-longest matching. The only thing persisted is its epoch, `kb_meta.gazetteer_epoch`, raised by `Gazetteer::bump_epoch` when a pass added names, so lot 4 can re-read the sources scanned against an older list |
| Keys and packs are refreshed by the pass, not by opening the index | `maintenance::ensure_phonetic_keys` compares the encoder's signature (`fr-rules:2`, which carries the language) with `kb_meta.phonetic_version` and rewrites every alias key from its display form in one transaction when they differ; `maintenance::record_packs` stores the packs fingerprint. Neither runs on `IndexStore::open`, because the locale belongs to the client and the index does not know it. Lot 4 calls both once, before the first file of a pass |
| The extraction port | `EntityExtractor` (`id`, `version`, `extract_document`, `extract_workbook`, both empty by default) returns `Candidate`s - a name as written, a type, a role, a method, a locator, a count, never a passage. `CompositeExtractor` registers several; candidates two **different** extractors found at the same place (same locator and overlapping span, or the same folded name when there is no span) are merged and the more confident stays, the earlier registered one on a tie. No NLP dependency is added |

## Owner answers after KB lot 3 (10 October 2026)

| Subject | Decision |
| --- | --- |
| Fuzzy minimum word length | **4**, confirmed (`resolve::FUZZY_MIN_TOKEN_LEN`), not the 6 of master section 7. One constant for the resolver and for lots 6 and 9 |
| A surname alone in a file | In ingestion, "Dr Martin" records nothing unless exactly one entity of the same source answers to it. The recall cost (a document that only says "Dr Martin" is not found when asking about Pierre Martin) is accepted; lot 11's shadow measurement may reopen it |
| Personal identifiers are stored as a keyed hash | For an identifier scheme with `personal_key: true` (e-mail address, social security number, IBAN) the knowledge base stores a keyed hash of the reduced value, never the value, so the local index does not become a directory of such numbers. Equality is all matching needs. The key is a per-workstation secret kept outside the index file. Limit to state in the DPIA draft: a low-entropy number can be guessed by someone who holds both the index and the key. Schemes without the flag (invoice and order numbers) keep their value. Designed in lot 4 |

## Settled by KB lot 4, entities from documents (10 October 2026)

| Subject | Decision |
| --- | --- |
| What a pass does with the knowledge base | `indexing::run_with_knowledge(.., knowledge: Option<&mut KnowledgeContext>)` is the pass the application runs; `indexing::run` keeps its exact signature and is the pass as it was (`None`: no name is read, the source is registered as not extracted, as in lot 1). Per file, once every embedding exists, the names in the chunks already in memory are read and written in the same transaction as the chunks; after the last file the documents that are behind are read again from their stored text. The context is `&mut` and not `&` as the lot file wrote, because the gazetteer grows while the pass runs |
| `knowledge_mode` | A setting (`off` / `suggest` / `auto`, default **`suggest`**), edited in `settings.json` until the dialog gets its "Advanced" group; the application has to be restarted to see a change. `off` is the kill switch: a context whose mode is `off` writes the documents with `IndexStore::replace_document_plain` and touches no `kb_*` row (it only *deletes* the source of a document that is analysed again, so knowledge never outlives the text it described). `auto` behaves like `suggest` until lot 6 |
| **The knowledge step never blocks indexing (lot 1's last open point)** | `replace_document_with_knowledge` applies the delta in a **savepoint** of the document's transaction. A failure rolls back the knowledge alone, forgets the source's previous knowledge (best effort, in a savepoint of its own), commits the chunks and returns `KnowledgeWrite::Bypassed`; the pass counts it in `knowledge.errors` and says so in the summary. The source is left without a row, so it is due and the next pass reads it again. **Two lot 1 tests encoded the old all-or-nothing rule and were rewritten** (`a_knowledge_step_that_fails_is_bypassed_and_the_document_is_still_indexed`, `a_first_write_with_a_failing_knowledge_step_still_indexes_the_document`); a delta for another file is still refused before anything is written. A document whose names could not be looked up (a failed read) is bypassed whole: a partial delta would erase what the previous version had rightly recorded |
| What the deterministic text extractor reads | In the order they win when they overlap: a name the base already holds in full (gazetteer, 0.90); an organisation with a legal-form suffix (0.80, active); an organisation after a prefix word such as "Association" (0.70, **candidate**); a title and a name (0.85 with two or more words, 0.70 with a surname alone); a surname or initial the gazetteer knows (0.70, only ever a link); two or three capitalised words not opening a sentence (0.40, candidate); identifiers by the packs' patterns (1.00); words of the file's own name that match a known name in full (0.50, a link, never a new entity). A month, a weekday or a document word (stop-words of the packs) is never a name; a run of four capitalised words is a heading; a capital that only opens a sentence is no evidence; a unit glued to digits is a wall. Everything language-specific comes from the packs |
| A surname alone creates nobody | Owner decision of 10 October 2026, applied: "Dr Martin" is linked to a person **this document** names in full when exactly one answers to the surname, and records nothing otherwise, even when a Martin is known from another file. **This overrides the lot file's "a single surname -> entity candidate"** (written before the decision). An initial and a surname (`J. Dupont`) links the same way, and otherwise becomes a separate candidate |
| Order inside a document | Whole names first, short forms second, file-name words last; the entities this document creates are not in the store yet, so the resolver reads the store through a layer that also shows it the drafts made so far (`ingest::Layered`), and `source_entities` holds the ids of both. `PossibleMatch` creates a separate entity and a `kb_possible_matches` row in the same delta (`KnowledgeDelta::possible_matches`); a fuzzy match is never a merge |
| Candidates and promotion | An automatic candidate with mentions in two distinct sources becomes active by one statement run after every delta (`store::promote_candidates`). There is **no demotion**: a promoted entity stays active if one of its two sources goes. Candidates never enter the gazetteer and never drive anything |
| Limits | 500 distinct names and 5 000 mentions per source, the most confident kept, and the file counted in `knowledge.truncatedSources`. A chunk a machine read below **0.80** (`MIN_OCR_CONFIDENCE_TO_CREATE`; the engine itself refuses a page under 0.6) creates nothing, no name and no identifier; it can only repeat a name the base knows, with the confidence scaled by the page's. Text that is empty, digits only, in another script or a broken PDF yields nothing and never an error; a panic in an extractor is caught and counted as a bypass |
| Personal identifiers | Hashed as the owner decided. `knowledge::secret::IdentifierKey`: 32 random bytes in `knowledge-identifier.key` in the application's local data folder (outside the index), `HMAC-SHA256(key, "acai.identifier.v1" 0 scheme 0 reduced value)`, 64 hex digits as the entity name and as the `identifier:<scheme>` value; the label is the kind and six digits of the hash; no alias, mention or log holds the value. A changed key (`kb_meta.identifier_key_check`) raises the gazetteer epoch so every document is read again and the old hashes are collected. What it protects and what it does not is written in `docs/PRIVACY-AND-SECURITY.md` and goes in the DPIA draft. `hmac` and `getrandom` become direct dependencies (both already in the tree, through `zip` and the TLS stack): no new code in the build, one line each in `Cargo.lock`. No `cfg`, no platform-specific file permission |
| Which packs are active | `health-fr` is on by default for the pilot (owner answer of 11 October 2026); the list is the setting `knowledge_packs`, edited with one tick per country in the settings dialog; **any number of countries can be on together** (a cross-border worker). The base is always loaded. Activity profiles that propose a pack by themselves are lot 10 |
| The epoch maintains itself | `gazetteer_epoch` is raised when the packs or the identifier key changed, when the list of known names grew during a pass, and when it is bigger at the start of a pass than the count recorded in `kb_meta.gazetteer_entries` (names added by a manual edit or by another pass, which is how lots 5 and 8 need no extra call). A source is due when it has no row, a row for other content, an older `kb_version`, or an older epoch (`store::documents_due`, one join). A refresh makes at most four rounds, because reading can teach the list something that makes the earlier documents behind again; what is left is picked up by the next pass |
| A refresh never overwrites a newer document | `IndexStore::apply_knowledge_refresh` compares the document's hash with the one the delta was read from, inside the transaction, and drops the write as `Stale` otherwise, so a document replaced while it is being read is never overwritten by an older reading |
| The refresh is local, and runs only inside an Analyse | `backfill::refresh_documents` holds no gateway client, opens no original file, and is asked between two documents whether to stop. It runs **only at the end of an Analyse the user started**: the start-up refresh of the first version of this lot (a Tauri command called by the interface once per launch) was **removed on the owner's instruction of 11 October 2026**, because a long silent task at launch freezes the interface without explanation. `knowledge_refresh` and `cancel_knowledge_refresh` no longer exist. An index analysed before the knowledge base, or while it was off, is therefore filled by the next Analyse the user presses (unchanged files are skipped by the pass but read for names from the stored text, with no request to the AI). **Not wired here: the end of `index_data_folder`** (lot 5 calls the same function) |
| The summary | `KnowledgeSummary` (counts only): people and organisations detected, how many the base already held, how many are new and of those how many candidates, **identifiers counted apart** (an invoice number is not "a person or an organisation"), documents read again, files cut at the caps, files whose names could not be recorded, milliseconds spent reading names. The lines are the interface's (`analysis.knowledge.*`); Rust builds no sentence |


## Knowledge packs are named domain-country (owner instruction, 11 October 2026)

| Subject | Decision |
| --- | --- |
| Who the product serves | French GPs (the pilot) **and Swiss health professionals**, whose patients have an **AVS / AHV number** (13 digits, prefix 756, written `756.1234.5678.97`). Binding for every agent, written in `AGENTS.md` ("Markets") |
| **The naming rule** | A pack id is **`<domain>-<country>`**, lower case: `health-fr`, `health-ch`, `legal-fr`, `accounting-fr`, and later `health-eu`, `legal-ch`, `legal-eu`... `domain` is what the vocabulary is about (`health`, `legal`, `accounting`), `country` is the jurisdiction whose rules and numbers it follows (`fr`, `ch`; `eu` is a region, kept for the European module). A bare `health` is not an id any more. `knowledge::packs::is_domain_country_id` checks the form, `COUNTRY_CODES` lists the codes a build knows, and `every_optional_pack_is_named_domain_dash_country` fails on an id that does not follow it. A new country is one more entry in the list |
| **Packs are separate files, and cumulative at run time** | Structure and behaviour are two different things. **Structure:** a module for another country is **another pack**, never a switch or an addition inside one (`health-ch` is not `health-fr` plus a flag), each named `<domain>-<country>` and each complete on its own (its own numbers, its own organisations, the patient and practitioner vocabulary), so the modules stay ordered and nothing is tangled. **Behaviour:** the packs are **cumulative**, not exclusive: the settings dialog has one tick per country (« Santé : France », « Santé : Suisse »), nothing ticked is no health module, and both can be on, because a French cross-border worker in Switzerland often has **both a French social security number and an AVS number**. A later `health-eu` is added the same way and removes nothing. The price is some duplicated vocabulary between `health-fr` and `health-ch` in French (words of the language, not of the country); the later fix is a language layer under the country packs |
| **A module never excludes or transforms an entity type (owner, 11 October 2026)** | *A module improves the interpretation and the detection of entities; it never removes an entity or changes its type. A person stays a person and an organisation stays an organisation, whatever modules are active.* Binding for every agent and for every future pack and knowledge provider. Applied as follows: (1) the **type vocabulary** of every shipped pack, that is its titles and its organisation markers, is **always read** (`PackSet::load_with_type_vocabulary`, used by every Analyse), active or not, so "CPAM du Rhone" is an organisation with no module, with the Swiss one, with the French one or with the European one; (2) what a module switches on is only what **only it can read**: its identifier patterns (the French or the Swiss insurance number), its column vocabulary, its role labels, its stop-words and its detection terms; (3) a test runs the same document under no module, each module, both and another domain's, and fails if the people and organisations found differ (`a_module_never_changes_the_type_of_a_name`). **Known gap:** with a name that no pack's vocabulary knows, the untitled-run rule (KBD-11) can still file an organisation as a person, whatever the modules; the fix belongs to that defect, not to a module |
| Language is not country | The locale of a pack **file** (`fr-FR.json`, `en-US.json`) is the language the words are written in; the pack **id** carries the country. **Known debt:** the `en-US` files of the `-fr` packs still hold US patterns (`ssn`, `ein`, the US case number) from lot 3. They belong to future `health-us`, `accounting-us`, `legal-us` packs and are to be moved there when a US module is wanted; nothing reads them in the pilot |
| Names a setting may still hold | `health`, `legal`, `accounting` written by an earlier build are read as the `-fr` pack (`canonical_pack_id`), and saved back under the new name |
| The pack list is a setting | `Settings.knowledge_packs` (default `["health-fr"]`), edited in « Réglages » -> « Module santé »; a change changes `kb_meta.packs_hash`, so the next Analyse (never anything in the background) reads the documents again. Done in lot 4 |
| One generic term | Whatever the country, an insurance number of a person is a **health insurance number** (*numero d'assurance-maladie*): the French NIR and the Swiss AVS number are entities of kind `health_insurance_number`, stored like every personal identifier as a keyed hash. Nothing downstream names a country. The role labels of the interface (`knowledge.roles.health.*`) are by domain, not by country |
| What `health-ch` holds | The AVS / AHV number, the Swiss IBAN and telephone, the Swiss insurer vocabulary (LAMal, caisse maladie, Krankenkasse, TARMED), plus the health vocabulary of the language (patient, practitioner, medication columns, titles, stop-words, roles). It holds no French number, and `health-fr` holds no Swiss number; the French organisations (`cpam`) are read in every case through the type vocabulary |
| Regulation | Each module follows its country's rules (Swiss: nLPD / FADP rather than the GDPR alone, the cantonal authority). The DPIA draft is written **per country** before real documents of that country are used (`AGENTS.md`, rule 6). Not designed in this lot |
| Limit | The AVS pattern checks the shape (756 prefix, 13 digits), not the EAN-13 check digit |
| **Direction (owner, 11 October 2026; not built)** | Packs will become **knowledge providers**: an API of knowledge sources and packs per domain and country (`SwissMedicalPack`, `FrenchMedicalPack`, `EuropeanMedicalPack`; for lawyers `SwissLegalPack` / `legal-ch`, `FrenchLegalPack` / `legal-fr`, `EuropeanLegalPack` / `legal-eu`), with the product opened to legal professionals later. Today a pack is a JSON file read by `knowledge::packs`; a provider would implement the same read-only interface, with the same naming (`<domain>-<country>` is the stable id, `FrenchMedicalPack` the code name). Whoever adds one keeps the rules above: one pack per country, nothing mixed, words and patterns only, and an external source never creates entities by itself (master, section 13). Do not build the API before the pilot holds (`docs/PLATFORM-VISION.md`) |

## Settled by KB lot 5, entities from tables (11 October 2026)

| Subject | Decision |
| --- | --- |
| What the Data pass does with the knowledge base | `data_folder::analyse_with_knowledge(.., knowledge: Option<&mut KnowledgeContext>)` is the pass the application runs; `analyse` keeps its exact signature and is the pass as it was. After a workbook's inventory is built (`tabular::build_inventory_with_workbook` keeps the parsed `Workbook`; `build_inventory` delegates and every existing caller is unchanged), its columns are read for what they mean and its values for the names, organisations and identifiers they hold, and the inventory and that knowledge are written in **one transaction** (`IndexStore::put_tabular_inventory_with_knowledge`). The knowledge part is applied in a **savepoint**, as for a document (owner rule of 9 October): a failure rolls back the knowledge alone, the inventory is committed, the workbook is left due and the pass counts it in `knowledge.errors`. **The lot file's "a failure after the inventory write rolls both back" is replaced by that rule**, as lot 4 did for documents. The engine is untouched: the inventory stored is byte for byte the one a pass without the knowledge base stores (`the_inventory_is_the_same_with_the_knowledge_base_on_and_off`), and no source of `knowledge/` so much as names the engine's sheet restriction (`the_knowledge_layer_never_writes_sheet_restrictions`) |
| Which sheets are read | The sheets the engine would itself answer from (`SheetInventory::looks_tabular`) and that have a header row. A notes sheet or a sheet made of formulas is not a table; a sheet with no header row has made-up column names (`column_3`) that say nothing, so none of its columns is classified. A formula cell is read as empty and counted: what a formula computed is a claim the file makes, never a name to remember. The cells of a date column are not even copied |
| How a header is read | `knowledge::extract::table`. The header is split into folded words (`packs::header_words`: separators, accents, case and camel case, so `Patient`, `nom_patient`, `NomPatient` and `Nom du patient` are alike). **An exact entry of the active packs wins at the pack's confidence; otherwise an entry the header contains, whose other words are particles or words of headers of the same entity type, is read at 0.9 of the pack's confidence** (`statut client` is nothing, `patient_name_full` is a patient). The lot file's flat 0.97 / 0.85 / 0.50 are replaced by the pack's own number per entry times 1.0 / 0.9, because a pack entry already carries a confidence (`client` 0.8, `reference` 0.7); the third tier (a single generic word such as `nom` in a longer header, 0.50) is below the 0.60 acceptance line, so it is the same as "not a match" and is not computed. `kb_column_semantics` holds **every column of a sheet that has a header row**: the ones that were read, with the reason (`header_exact`, `header_partial`, `value_shape`, `combined`), and the ones that were not, with theirs (`no_header_match`, `module_off`, `ambiguous_header`, `number_or_date`, `no_values`, `values_mismatch`; see "Owner answers after KB lot 5"). A reader that wants the readings only filters on `ColumnSemanticsRow::is_read` (a reading reason and a confidence of at least 0.60) |
| **A module never changes a type, for tables too (owner, 11 October 2026)** | Two checks, both tested. (1) A header is looked up in the active packs **and** in the vocabulary of every pack the build ships; if the two read it as different entity types the column is left alone (`Vocabulary`, `classify_column`). (2) A header that merely contains a vocabulary word counts only when its other words belong to the same kind of thing. So with the module that reads `patient` off, the column is **not classified**: it carries less meaning, it is never read as something else. A header the base reads (`fournisseur`, `client`, `nom`, `entreprise`) gives the same type with no module at all. `a_module_never_changes_the_type_of_a_column` runs every header of every shipped pack, in both languages, under all sixteen combinations of the optional packs, and fails on any type that differs from the header's; `a_module_never_changes_the_type_of_a_name_in_a_table` does the same on two workbooks end to end. **Switching a module on or off makes the workbooks due again** (the vocabulary changed: `kb_meta.vocabulary_epoch`), and a workbook read again with less simply knows less; nothing it knew is turned into something else |
| What the values may refuse | A sample of up to 200 non-empty cells decides whether the values look like what the header says. A **person** column needs 70 % of names (one to four words of letters, with a hyphen, an apostrophe, a period or one comma, no digit, no organisation marker); an **identifier** column needs 80 % of cells that are, whole, an identifier of an active scheme; any other column needs 70 % of short texts (80 characters, 6 words, with a letter). Under the share the confidence is lowered in proportion (`confidence x share / required`) and a column that falls below 0.60 is rejected. A column typed `Numeric` or `Date` by the inventory is never a column of names; an identifier column may be numeric. A column with **no** usable header and no vocabulary entry in any pack is read only as an identifier column, when at least five values are all (90 %) identifiers of an active scheme: reason `value_shape`, confidence 0.65. The pilot's mail-merge file gives `No_Commande` this way |
| Which values become entities | The distinct, trimmed values of a kept column. Skipped: empty cells, a cell without a letter or a digit (`-`, `?`), a pack null-like word (`N/A`, `NULL`, `unknown`, `inconnu`, a month, `total`), a pure number (in a column of names), a cell longer than 80 characters or 6 words (free text), and anything that is not shaped like the column (an organisation marker in a column of people). 20 000 distinct values per column and 50 000 candidates per workbook, the first ones kept and the workbook counted in `knowledge.truncatedSources`. Titles in front of a name (`Dr Bruno Baker`) are read and not part of the name. `unknown` was added to the French stop-words (a French export writes it) |
| One mention per entity, sheet, column and role | Never one row per cell. The mention holds the number of cells, the **first row** and up to **three sample rows**, all the **rows of the file as the spreadsheet program numbers them** (the header of a plain table is row 1, its first data row is row 2; a title row or two empty rows above the header shift them; see "Owner answers after KB lot 5"). A person written two ways in one column is one mention with the rows of both. The method is always `column_value` (the `Method` enum's code; the lot file wrote `column_semantics`), and it is **never rewritten** by a later round that reads documents against the names the pass taught: `KBD-13` is not copied, and a test reads the method after a documents refresh |
| A given name and a family name | A sheet with a given-name column and a family-name column (`Prenom`, `Nom`) gives **one person per row**, `Jean Dupont`, and neither column gives an entity on its own: a surname alone is not a person (owner, 10 October 2026) and a first name alone is not worth remembering. A row missing one of the two gives nobody. The mention sits on the family-name column. A sheet with only a family-name column reads its values as names, whole or surname alone |
| Resolution | The same `DeltaBuilder` as for a document (`ingest.rs`), not a copy: `place_table` lifts the document builder's resolution, creation and mention code into one more entry point. Identifiers first, then names of more than one word, then single words, so that a surname alone can be linked to a person this very workbook names in full. The resolver runs in ingestion mode with `Method::ColumnValue`, so a value nothing else knows is a new **active** entity (the column is the evidence; there are no candidates from tables), an exact name or alias is the same entity as the one a document gave (`Jean Dupont` in a letter and in a table is one entity), and a spelling that only sounds alike is a separate entity with a `kb_possible_matches` row. A deleted name (tombstone) is not recreated. A surname alone that a person of the store carries and this workbook does not settle records nothing, as in a document |
| Relations | Only from the pack's `relations` list, only between two role columns of **one sheet**, once per distinct pair of values with the number of rows that state it (`client -> provider: served_by`, `client -> supplier: supplied_by`, `sender -> recipient: sent_to`, `client -> counterparty: opposed_to`). Nothing is inferred from two names being in one file (`two_people_in_a_document_make_no_relation`). With the module that gives a column its role off, the columns carry no role and state no relation. 10 000 relations per workbook |
| Identifiers | An identifier column is read cell by cell with the schemes of the active packs, the scheme named by the column's subtype first. An identifier of a `personal_key` scheme (an e-mail address, a social security number, an AVS number, an IBAN) is **hashed exactly as in `ingest.rs`** (`IdentifierKey::digest`, label = the kind and six digits) and never written as itself: the same address in a letter and in a table is one entity. When a sheet has **exactly one column of people**, such an identifier on the same row is an attribute `identifier:<scheme>` of that person, whose value is the hash; with two person columns (a client and a provider) the table does not say whose number it is, and nothing is attached. Written in `docs/PRIVACY-AND-SECURITY.md`; the management dialog of lot 8 must mark these attributes sensitive |
| What is remembered for the scan of documents | The names of **people and organisations** only (`gazetteer::is_listed_type`). A value of an item or a place column is remembered by the base and counted apart, but it is not a phrase to look for in a letter (`Lyon`, `Stylo` are words of every document). The same rule now builds the list at the start of a pass and teaches it during one; before it the list took every non-identifier entity, which a pass over a table would have filled with article names. The summary counts people and organisations only in `entitiesDetected`; places, items and terms are counted apart in the store's counts (`SourceEntityCounts::others`) and not shown |
| When a workbook is read again | A pass rebuilds every inventory, as before. The knowledge of a workbook is read again only when its content changed, when the extractor is newer, when the vocabulary changed (packs or identifier key), or when it was left due by a failure. **Not** when the list of names grew: a table does not depend on it (`kb_meta.vocabulary_epoch`, recorded with the epoch of the pass that read the workbook). A second pass over unchanged workbooks writes nothing and reads nothing (`a_second_pass_over_unchanged_workbooks_writes_nothing_and_reads_nothing`, a snapshot of every `kb_` table) |
| The documents read again after a Data pass | When the workbooks taught the base names the documents were not read against, the end of the Data pass runs `backfill::refresh_documents` (lot 4's function, unchanged): local, from the stored text, no request, and the progress bar says "Lecture des noms dans les documents deja analyses" (the French string of the interface). It runs inside the Analyse the user started and nowhere else (`AGENTS.md`, "No costly silent background work"); there is no start-up task |
| A workbook with no usable sheet, an unreadable one, a pass with extraction off | The inventory is written as ever and what an earlier version of the workbook taught the base is dropped (a delete); nothing is written to a `kb_` table. Switching the mode back on reads the workbooks again |
| The summary | `KnowledgeSummary` gains `semanticColumns` and `workbooksAnalysed` (counts only); the Data card's pass summary prints the knowledge lines (people and organisations, identifiers, columns recognised in how many spreadsheets, links read between columns). A workbook that was not read again is not counted |
| Added to the packs | A national social security number column for `health-fr` (`NIR`, `numero de securite sociale`, `SSN` in English; subtype `health_insurance_number`, 0.95), so a column of such numbers is an identifier column where the French module is on and nothing where it is off. `unknown` in the French base stop-words |
| Left for later | A header in another script. The AVS check digit. A type for a column that mixes people and organisations (the management dialog of lot 8 corrects it by hand) |

## Owner answers after KB lot 5 (11 October 2026)

| Subject | Decision |
| --- | --- |
| **KBD-15, the surname-then-initial form** | Fixed **before lot 6**, in its own branch `fix/kb-surname-initial` from `kb/integration` after lot 5 is merged: `aliases_for` reads a name whose last word is a single letter (`Dupont J`) as surname then initial, and the resolver offers `Jean Dupont` as a possible match. Not a blocker of lot 5 |
| **Headers are read in both languages** | A header is read in the words of every language the build ships, whatever the interface language: an export from an English program is read in a French session, and the other way round. The active modules' rules are loaded for the other languages once per set of modules (`TableEntityExtractor::rules_for`), and the vocabulary check that keeps a module from changing a type now covers every pack **and** every language. Entries of different types at the same rank (two languages, two packs) are a **conflict**: the column is not read (`ambiguous_header`), never a pick. `a_module_never_changes_the_type_of_a_column` still runs every header of every pack under all sixteen combinations |
| **Columns that were not read are recorded, with the reason** | `no_header_match` (no pack, in any language, has a word for it: `salle`, `montant`, a date), `module_off` (a module that is off would read it; the type it would have is kept as a hint with confidence 0, never as a reading), `ambiguous_header`, `number_or_date` (the header means a name and the column holds numbers or dates), `no_values`, `values_mismatch` (the header means something and the values do not look like it; the lowered confidence is kept). The summary's `semanticColumns` counts the readings only; the entities come from the readings only. The management dialog of lot 8 answers "why was this column not read" from this table; `show_kb_entities.py` prints both lists |
| **Rows are the rows of the file** | A mention's first row and sample rows are the numbers the spreadsheet program shows. The adapters report them with the workbook: `tabular::SourceRows` (sheet name to the source row of each row), from `open_with_source_rows` of the CSV and XLSX adapters, which `open` delegates to, so the engine is unchanged. XLSX: the first used cell's row (`calamine`'s range start) plus the index; a sheet that starts on row 3 is numbered from 3. CSV: the line each record starts on, counted from the bytes (the CSV reader skips a blank line without counting it, and reports a record's position at the start of the blank lines before it), so a blank line keeps its number and a field spanning lines pushes the next row down. `workbook_input` aligns them with the data rows; without them a row is numbered from 1 among the data rows |
| **Order numbers** | The base packs gain header entries for an order number (`No commande`, `n° commande`, `numero de commande`, `bon de commande`; `order number`, `PO number`, `purchase order`...) with subtype `order`, and the `order_number` pattern accepts `CMD-2026-001` whole (a number with a year and a sequence). In a document the same text is still read as an invoice number when both patterns match the same span (the first scheme of the pack wins a tie); in a column the header names the scheme |
| **A `Nom` column of company names** | Accepted: the type comes from the header, never from a guess about the values (the owner's rule on modules). A value that carries an organisation marker (`SARL`, `Association`) is still refused in a column of people. Correction by hand in lot 8 |
| **Upper-case names** | `DUPONT Jean` is shown `Dupont Jean`, as for documents. Kept |
| **Items and places** | Remembered by the base, not scanned for in documents (`gazetteer::is_listed_type`). Kept; a manual entity of such a type typed in lot 8 needs a rule there if it should be scanned |

## Settled by the fix "surname then initial" (KBD-15, 11 October 2026)

| Subject | Decision |
| --- | --- |
| **The form** | `Dupont J`, `Dupont J.`, `DUPONT J`, `de Vries A`: a person's name whose **last word is one letter** after exactly one word of identity (particles allowed in front). Decided in one place, `normalize::surname_then_initial`, which aliases and resolution both ask. It is narrow on purpose: `J. Dupont` (initial then surname), `A B`, `J. P.`, `Jean Dupont J` (a middle initial) and one-word names are **not** this form and keep their behaviour. The cost of the narrowness is a given name followed by one letter (`Jean M`) being read as surname `Jean`; no source can tell, and the result is only ever a question, never a link |
| **Aliases** | The surname alone is a `partial_surname`; `J. Dupont` is the one `initial` alias (the reordered form `J Dupont` normalises to the same text, so it is not a second alias); the title forms of the titles seen. **No one-letter surname, no `D. J`.** The initial alias keeps the kind `initial` on purpose: kept as `reordered` it would read as a named variant and `J. Dupont` would resolve to `Dupont J` alone instead of staying a question beside `Jean Dupont` |
| **Resolution** | `Resolver::initial_compatibility`, after the exact name and alias and before the fuzzy rule: a surname and an initial against the full names that carry the same surname and whose given name starts with that letter, **whichever of the two is stored and whichever is read**. At ingestion: `PossibleMatch`, reason `initial`, `create_separate: true`, one `kb_possible_matches` row per full name, never a link (master section 7). In a question: `PossibleMatch` for one full name, `Ambiguous` for several (`Jean Dupont`, `Jeanne Dupont`), never a pick, and nothing that creates. A person only; an organisation or an item keeps its rules |
| **What this does not do** | A question for the exact full name `Jean Dupont` still resolves to that entity (an exact name wins). Widening a reduction to the files of its possible matches is lot 6's decision; the pair is in `kb_possible_matches` for it. A surname alone (`Dupont`) now asks when both forms are in the selection, which is the intended effect |
| **Existing data: the alias generation, not the extractor version** | `CURRENT_KB_VERSION` is **not** raised: re-reading a source finds its people already known and would not remake their aliases, and it would read every document again for nothing. Instead `kb_meta.alias_generation` (`maintenance::ALIAS_GENERATION`, now 2) is checked when an Analyse builds its knowledge context, next to the phonetic keys: once, in one transaction, the automatic aliases of the people written surname then initial are brought to the current rules (the wrong ones deleted, the right ones written, titles seen kept, manual aliases never touched) and the possible matches with their full names are recorded. It reads no document and runs only inside an Analyse the user started. Raise `ALIAS_GENERATION` and add the correction to `ensure_alias_generation` whenever `aliases_for` changes for entities that already exist |

## Settled by KB lot 6, targeted scope (11 October 2026)

| Subject | Decision |
| --- | --- |
| Where it lives | `knowledge::query` (the detector), `knowledge::reduce` (the reducer and the shadow comparison) and `knowledge::targeting` (the plan, the search, the report, the workbook narrowing), all free of Tauri, so the whole question path below the window is tested without one (`tests/knowledge_targeting.rs`, `tests/knowledge_isolation.rs`). `commands.rs` only reads the settings and the folders and forwards events. `targeting.rs` is not in the lot file's list: the orchestration had to live somewhere a test can reach |
| What a question names | `QueryEntityDetector`: no model, no gateway call. Chips are resolved by definition (confidence 1.0). Runs of one to five words, longest first, are looked up in the base with **one** query (`store::known_name_forms`) before the resolver of lot 3 is asked, in query mode and **restricted to the selection** (invariant I7: two Martins in the base and one in the selection is a resolved Martin). A capitalised word of four letters or more that no run explained goes to the fuzzy rule and can only become a **possible match**, never a resolved entity. Identifiers of the active packs' schemes are matched in the text; a personal one needs the identifier key, otherwise it is left alone. A question of eight words or fewer that names nobody carries the previous answer's entities, held by the client (confidence 0.85, a candidate stays capped at 0.40). Single words that are stop-words, numbers or shorter than three letters are not names, and dates and months stay with the tabular engine |
| What drives a reduction | A resolved entity of confidence 0.80 or more that is not a candidate and whose share of the selected files is at most `knowledge_max_selectivity` (0.6). Decided per side: an entity only a workbook holds leaves the documents alone. A **chip** drives a reduction at any size and whatever its share (explicit user intent), and a chip is the answer to an ambiguity. **Any ambiguity inside the selection stops the reduction** (`ambiguous`), not only one on "the main span" as the lot file says: the question has no main span a rule can find, and a name left out of the reduction is a file lost. Possibles are offered, never applied |
| Which files are searched | The files of the driving entities **in the selection**, ranked by how many of the entities each holds (no hard intersection), then confidence, then path. Added: (1) **the files of the open possible matches** of a driving entity (KBD-15, decided here: `Dupont J` and `Jean Dupont` are the same person until the user says otherwise, so a reduction for one keeps the files of the other; `Combien de rendez-vous pour Jean Dupont` still reaches `rdv-mars-2026.xlsx`); (2) **the lexical safety net**, documents only: the files of the selection whose text holds a form of the name (all its aliases, quoted as phrases, two letters or more, `IndexStore::files_matching_terms_in`), which is what reaches `M. Dupont` and `Jean-Dupont`; a surname alone or a title and a surname is searched **only when no other entity of the base carries it**, otherwise `martin` would pull in every other Martin and undo the reduction; (3) **every file the base has not read**: a file with no source row under its current content, or read by no extractor, cannot be left out because of what the base knows. The cap `knowledge_max_candidates` (15) applies to the first two groups, documents only, and the unread files are added beyond it. An empty first group is `no_candidates`: the old path on the full selection |
| Skip reasons | `off`, `user_widened`, `named_file`, `small_scope`, `no_entity`, `no_discriminating_entity`, `ambiguous`, `no_candidates`, `insignificant` (less than `knowledge_min_reduction` = 30 % of the files dropped; for a chip, any drop), `lookup_error`. The lot file's list plus `no_entity` (the question names nothing the base knows, kept apart from `no_discriminating_entity`) and `user_widened` (the widen action). One test each, `tests/knowledge_targeting.rs` |
| What each mode does | `off`: no lookup, no `kb_diagnostics` row. `suggest`: the question is read and the reduction computed and measured, **the search is the one that runs with the base off** (`suggest_searches_exactly_as_off_does_and_only_measures`, same evidence, same order, same scores), nothing is reported to the interface except names to ask about; a **chip** applies. `auto`: the reduction applies, the other spellings of the resolved names are added to the **lexical** part of the search only (the embedding text stays the question), and a report is sent. The widen action (`targeting.mode = off`) is the old path, no expansion either |
| Widening | When a reduction was **applied**, the search is repeated once on the full selection if it found nothing or, for a ranked search, if the closest passage **by meaning alone** is under `knowledge_widen_below_score`. The lexical bonus is left out on purpose: a reduced set was chosen for the name it holds, so its excerpts always carry the bonus and a score with the bonus says nothing about the rest of the question (`SearchOutcome::best_vector_score`; the lot file said "best score"). For an "each document" question only an empty result widens (a list built for coverage has low scores by nature). **Measured value 0.65**, on `nomic-embed-text` through the gateway (`lots/lot-06-widen-calibration.py`): questions about nothing in the passages reach 0.555 to 0.645, questions whose passage is in the reduced set reach 0.698 to 0.843. **Limit, found by the measurement:** a question that names a person whose files do not hold the answer scores 0.67 to 0.76 against those files, in the range of a good match, so no absolute score catches it. The score protects against a reduced set that is *unrelated*; what protects against a related-but-wrong one is the lexical net, the unread files, the possible matches, the visible widen button (lot 7) and the shadow measurement that decides, at lot 11, whether `auto` becomes the default. The scale belongs to the embedding model: change the model, run the script again |
| Workbooks | The workbook side is decided from the usable workbooks of the selection, keyed by their workbook id. A question that names a workbook (`names_a_workbook`, the file resolver on the selected records) is never narrowed. `suggest`: `TabularAnswer::WhichWorkbook` gains `hints` (machine data: the file, the entities of the question it holds, the sheets where they appear) and its buttons are ordered, workbooks the base points at first; the choice stays hers. `auto`: the engine is given `narrowed_data_selection`, a shorter list **built from the user's own entries** (or, for "all workbooks", from the folder's records), keeping each entry's sheets, so it cannot contain a workbook she did not choose; one candidate answers directly and the report says which and why (`chosen_workbook`). The engine, its inputs beyond that list and the sheet restrictions are untouched (I5): the engine answers from the narrowed selection exactly as it answers when she picks that workbook herself (`the_engine_answers_the_same_from_the_narrowed_selection_as_from_the_user_naming_the_workbook`). `mixed_answer::entity_link` is not touched |
| Diagnostics | `kb_diagnostics` (counts, codes and milliseconds; a ring of 2 000 rows) gains `path`, `applied`, `would_apply`, `reduction_percent` and the three miss counters, added to a table made by lot 1 by `ensure_schema`. For each cited file outside the reduced set the miss is classified `no_mention_in_cited_file` (an extraction gap), `capped`, or `entity_not_resolved` (the file does mention an entity of the question that was not used). `RetrievalDiagnostics.targeting` carries the same in the answer and in `retrieval-timings.jsonl`: entities as id, type code, confidence and method, never a name. `knowledge_diagnostics(limit)` adds the rows up (questions, share with entities, would-apply rate, median reduction, recall overall and for would-apply questions, misses by reason, skips by reason). A question in `off` mode leaves no row. The I3 snapshot leaves out `kb_diagnostics` and nothing else |
| Settings | `knowledge_min_scope_files` 8, `knowledge_max_selectivity` 0.6, `knowledge_min_reduction` 0.3, `knowledge_max_candidates` 15, `knowledge_widen_below_score` 0.65 (`serde(default)`, clamped on the way out and on save, `Settings::targeting_policy`). `Settings` loses `Eq` (it holds floats). `knowledge_hide_names_in_suggestions` belongs to lots 7 and 9 and is not added here. Edited in `settings.json` until the settings dialog gets its Advanced group (lot 7) |
| Contracts | `ask_with_sources(.., targeting: Option<TargetingRequest>)`; `AskAnswer.targeting: Option<TargetingReport>`; `ChatStreamEvent::Targeting` sent just before `Sources`. TypeScript types only in `src/lib/ipc.ts` (the interface is lot 7); a stream event the interface does not know is ignored by its handler. The report names entities of the selection only (I7) and nothing outside it |
| The vocabulary is kept for the session | `AppState` keeps one `QueryVocabulary` (packs, titles, phonetic encoder) per locale and set of packs; loading it costs tens of milliseconds and happens on the first question, never at launch |
| A quadratic lookup fixed on the way | `store::source_ids_for_scope` (lot 3) joined the sources onto `json_each` directly, which can parse the whole array once per source: 542 ms for a selection of 1 000 files. The pairs are now read once into a materialised table (`sources_in_scope` is written the same way): 14 ms for 1 000. Same result, same order |
| KBD-12 (a promoted candidate is never demoted) | **Not changed, still deferred.** A name two files once agreed on is `active` and resolves above the candidate cap, but it can only narrow a scope through the files that still mention it, and the lexical net adds the files that hold its forms: the hole of master section 6 rule 5 does not become a recall loss here |
| Left for later | The targeting line, the widen button, the chips and the entity picker (lot 7). The suggestion names (lots 7 and 9). A relative widening rule (compare with a sample of the full selection) if the shadow data of lot 11 shows the absolute one misses too much. `chunks_for_document` scans the whole table for each file (no index on `chunks.relative_path`), which makes a search over hundreds of explicitly selected files quadratic (KBD-16) |

## Settled by KB lot 7, targeting in the interface (11 October 2026)

| Subject | Decision |
| --- | --- |
| What the user sees | A small line under an answer whose search was **narrowed** (never for a narrowing that was only measured or skipped, so in `suggest` the old screen is unchanged): « Recherche ciblée : 7 documents sur 40 — Jean Dupont » with **Élargir à ma sélection** and **Voir les fichiers**; the variant « à votre demande » when a chip drove it; « Recherche élargie à toute votre sélection : peu de résultats dans les documents ciblés. » when the narrowed search was repeated on everything (no action, the files can still be listed); « Classeur choisi : factures-2026.xlsx — Jean Dupont y figure » with **Choisir un autre classeur**. "Applied" is read from the **paths** of the report (`candidatePaths`, `workbookPaths`), never from `strategy`, which also says `entity_files` for a narrowing that was only measured. Every sentence is a catalogue key (`chat.targeting.*`) in both languages; Rust sends none |
| Widen is a re-ask, not an edit | **Élargir à ma sélection** and **Choisir un autre classeur** call `ask_with_sources` again with `targeting.mode = off` and replace the answer exactly as *Régénérer* does (`useChat.retarget`). A widened tabular question therefore comes back with the existing which-workbook buttons. The choice (`targetingChoice`) is kept on the new answer, so *Régénérer* on a widened answer is widened again; an edited and resent question starts clean |
| Which one? / did you mean? | Under the answer, **after** it has been given on the whole selection (an ambiguity never blocks): « Plusieurs personnes correspondent à « Dr Martin » : » with one button per name of the selection, each with its number of files, or « Vouliez-vous dire : » for a possible match. A button re-asks with `entityIds = [id]`, which is a chip, therefore an explicit narrowing. The words quoted are the **span** of the question the resolver settled on, exactly as typed (`J. Dupont`, not the folded form), carried by the report. Offers are dropped when fewer than two names remain for an ambiguity, when the user already picked that name on this answer, and **when a name counts no file of the selection** (the client's second line of defence for I7, `ambiguity_chips_only_offer_entities_present_in_the_selection` being the first, in Rust) |
| Candidates are never shown | A name the base has not confirmed (confidence 0.40 or less) is not quoted in the line, not carried to the next question and not offered by the picker (defect KBD-11, which asked lots 7 and 9 to hide candidates). The picker filters on `status = active` in Rust; the line filters on the confidence in the report |
| The `@` picker | `knowledge::picker::search_entities` and the command `knowledge_search_entities(prefix, scope, limit)`: the names of the **current selection** that begin with the typed letters (the store's prefix search, no minimum length), each with its documents and workbooks of the selection, at most 8. It resolves documents and workbooks exactly as a question does (inventory with the application's hash cache, `scope.resolve`, usable workbooks), so it offers what the next question could use. An empty prefix, an empty selection or the mode `off` find nothing: an empty selection must not become a way to read the base's names. Typing is debounced (120 ms), a slow answer to an old prefix is dropped, a failed lookup is an empty list and never an error: the picker cannot block a question. Keyboard: arrows (wrapping), Enter or Tab choose, Escape closes, and with nothing to choose Enter sends the question as usual. An `@` button next to the model choice starts the same list for whoever does not know the key |
| Chips | Choosing a name writes its display text into the question and adds a removable chip above the box. A chip is **an id** (`targeting.entity_ids`), never a sentence appended to the question (KBD-04): the model reads the plain text she wrote. Chips travel with the next send and clear; a stop that hands the question back hands the chips back; a change of the selection clears them (their counts were of the old selection) |
| The one outside-selection surface | `outsideSelection` of the same command: a name that has **no** file in the selection but files among the analysed ones, as `{ name, documents[], workbooks[] }`, at most 3 names and 50 paths a side. The picker shows one line « Jean Dupont figure dans 4 fichiers non sélectionnés » with **Ajouter à ma sélection**; the click is hers and goes through the existing ticking rules (`addScopeFiles` shares `scopeOfEntries` with `toggleScopeFile`; the ids are pinned from a fresh read of each folder; locked while an answer is being written). This is the only surface that may mention a name outside the selection (I7) |
| Carry-over | The client keeps the previous answer's confirmed names (`carryOverIds`) and sends them as `carryOver` with every question; Rust honours them only for a question of eight words or fewer that names nobody (lot 6). Nothing is stored by Rust and nothing is persisted by the client (I3). Only an answer whose report is present has any: in `suggest`, with no narrowing and no name to ask about, there is none |
