| Empty conversation | Left-aligned, vertically centred, no wider than an answer (78 %); title the size of « Suggestions », body text the size of an answer, no blank line between its two sentences, the show/hide names button the size of the conversation buttons |

## Taken while packaging the document viewer, to confirm (17 October 2026)

Part 02B of the workspace series (`feat/document-viewer`). The Rust side and the interface are other tabs; this one settled the packaging. Security text: `docs/PRIVACY-AND-SECURITY.md`, "The document viewer: content security policy and the no-network proof".

| Subject | Decision |
| --- | --- |
| The pinned library | `shrimp-doc-viewer` **0.5.0**, exact (no caret), with `@embedpdf/pdfium` 2.15.1 through the lockfile. **Risk accepted knowingly:** the library was created in June 2026, has seven releases in two months and one maintainer, and its type file is stale. Containment: it is imported in **one file** (`src/lib/viewerEngine.ts`, typed from its sources), the version is exact, and the Vite plugin below throws if the library changes the line it patches, so an upgrade is a deliberate act |
| Vite configuration | `optimizeDeps.exclude: ["shrimp-doc-viewer", "@embedpdf/pdfium"]` (dev pre-bundling would move the WebAssembly files and workers away from the `new URL(..., import.meta.url)` that finds them), `worker.format: "es"` (the workers are modules), and a short plugin that replaces the library's default-font expression with `"about:blank"` in dev and in build. The application always passes its own font, so the substitute is never fetched. Measured: without the plugin a viewer-including bundle holds the 7 149 196-byte `NotoSansTC-Regular.ttf` (14.15 MB in all); with it the font is absent (7.00 MB) |
| The font | **Noto Sans Regular only**, subset to Basic Latin, Latin-1, Latin Extended-A, general punctuation, the euro sign and a few symbols (437 glyphs, 33 092 bytes, no hinting), SIL OFL 1.1 (`src/assets/fonts/OFL.txt`). Bold, italic and bold italic were built (33 to 35 KB each) and **dropped**: measured on a real DOCX and on a built one, the engine **draws bold itself from the Regular outlines and does not draw italic at all**, and its `fonts` option only maps a font family named in a document to a file, so a styled file can never be chosen per run. Consequence, accepted: italic text shows upright, and serif or other families fall back to the one sans font |
| Non-Latin scripts | Chinese, Cyrillic, Arabic and every other script outside the subset render blank. Accepted and documented (owner, 17 October) |
| Content security policy | The two narrow additions and the separate `devCsp` described in the privacy document. `capabilities/default.json` unchanged |
| Notices | `apps/desktop/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md` (shrimp-doc-viewer, `@embedpdf/pdfium` and PDFium, the font's licence). The library's own 708 KB list of the 73 Rust crates compiled into its core is **referenced, not copied**: vendoring it before the first installer is the owner's call |
| Offline test in a browser engine | **GO**: every supported format opens from the owner's folders in Edge (Chromium 155, the WebView2 engine) under the release policy with module workers, no external request, no violation. The packaged window on WebView2, WKWebView and macOS is still unproven |

## Taken while building the viewer interface (17 October 2026)

Part 02C of the workspace series (`feat/document-viewer`). Behaviour: `docs/CLIENT.md`, "The document viewer".

| Subject | Decision |
| --- | --- |
| Who can be opened | Only an analysed file (`isSelectable`), from the sidebar lists of both folders, with an **Ouvrir** button independent of the checkbox. Opening never changes the selection and never analyses. A `.csv` or `.xlsx` in the Documents Folder is listed but not analysed, hence not openable: a limit of the two-folder model, kept (owner, 17 October) |
| The format map | From the extension, forced, never sniffed. Library: `pdf docx xlsx pptx odt ods odp rtf csv`, and `png jpg jpeg gif bmp webp svg` as `image`. The application's text view: `txt md srt vtt eml`. Unsupported (message plus the reveal action): everything else, `doc xls ppt tiff heic` included, never advertised, never converted |
| Text is shown by the application, not the library | The library's text engine joins single line breaks (a subtitle's index, timestamp and text on one line; a mail's headers run together). `TextView` shows the characters as stored, CRLF as one break, monospace, wrapping. Bounded at **500 000 characters**, cut at a line end with a visible notice, and only the first part of a large file is decoded |
| In-memory UTF-8 | Text-like files reach the viewer as UTF-8 from Rust (UTF-16 with a mark, else UTF-8 or Windows-1252). Nothing is rewritten on disk (`AGENTS.md`, rule 3). The 128 MiB cap stands |
| The library's toolbar is hidden, the controls are the application's | The toolbar's labels are in one fixed language (Chinese) and cannot be changed. It is the first child `div` of the host and sets its own `display`, so one rule with `!important` hides it. Controls are built from the handle members measured in the real window: zoom (own `zoomIn` / `zoomOut`, or a step of 1.25 through `setZoom` for images), `fitWidth`, `pageCount`, `sheetNames` with `setSheet`, `next` / `prev` with `slideCount`. Every label goes through both catalogues and follows the locale at once |
| Fit to width | Only for pages and pictures (`docx odt odp rtf image`), on opening and after a debounced resize, until the user zooms. Not for grids (an XLSX fitted to a wide zone opened at 255 %). The PDF refits itself. The zone is mounted only once it has a width |
| Image zoom | A picture grows with its zoom up to 150 % and not beyond (measured), so the application stops its step there instead of letting the number climb |
| Outside change | One check at window `focus` while a file is open: the inventory of that folder is read again and compared with the opened record (missing, no longer analysed, different hash). The centre closes and one single-button modal appears (`ConfirmDialog` `singleAction`, the two-answer behaviour unchanged). `viewer_file_missing` ends in the same modal. No watcher, no timer |
| Title-bar button | Unchanged: it toggles the welcome view and closes any open file; it does not reopen the last file. To decide with the owner |
| The checkpoint | Level 3 of the no-network proof: the 291-page PDF rendered in the real WebView2 window of a release build (`pnpm tauri build --no-bundle`) under the release policy. Wi-Fi off was stated by the owner for the first attempt |

## Taken while building the Markdown Raw / Preview switch (17 October 2026)

Part 03 of the workspace series (`feat/document-viewer`). Behaviour: `docs/CLIENT.md`, "The document viewer".

| Subject | Decision |
| --- | --- |
| Raw stays the default | A `.md` opens as stored, in `TextView`. The switch **Brut** \| **Aperçu** appears only for a file with more than one mode (`previewModes`: today `md` only). The mode lives in `FileViewer`'s state as a typed `PreviewMode`, so part 04 can lift it into a tab; it resets to raw when another file is opened, and scroll is not kept across the switch |
| The preview is the application's own `Markdown` component, not the library | Owner, 17 October. That component is already the chat's: no `rehype-raw`, and links and images are reduced to their text, so a link cannot navigate the Tauri window and an image cannot cause a request. Checked by rendering to a string in vitest: `[x](https://example.invalid)` gives its text, `![alt](https://example.invalid/x.png)` gives the alt text, a reference link, an autolink and a `file:///` path give no `<a>`, raw `<script>` and `<img>` stay text. `Markdown.tsx` is not changed; the preview adds a wrapper and scoped CSS (larger headings than in a conversation) |
| Same bounded text as Raw | The preview goes through `prepareViewerText` (500 000 characters, notice). Measured in Node (render only, no DOM): a mixed document takes about 0.3 s at 50 000 characters, 0.5 s at 100 000, 0.9 s at 200 000 and **4.4 to 5.3 s at 500 000**. The bound is kept as specified; a lower preview-only bound (100 000 to 200 000 characters) is proposed to the owner, not applied |

## Taken while building the readable previews of EML, SRT and VTT (18 October 2026)

Part 05 of the workspace series (`feat/readable-previews`). Behaviour: `docs/CLIENT.md`, "The document viewer".

| Subject | Decision |
| --- | --- |
| The second mode shows the readers' text | Owner, 17 October: exactly what the assistant indexes (`readers::eml`, `srt`, `vtt`), not a purpose-built rendering, so there is no second MIME parser to maintain and the user sees what the assistant reads. The readers and the indexing are untouched. The view omits what the readers omit (listed in the client document) |
| The label | Owner, 17 October: **Texte lu par l'assistant** for these three formats, so the text is not mistaken for the original. `.md` keeps **Aperçu**. The mode value stays `preview` in the tab state; the label depends on `previewKind` |
| EML scope | Whatever the reader does: no attachment list, no HTML rendering, no new decoding. More is a later decision |
| Other formats | A separate, later decision. `previewKind` is a table keyed by extension, so another format is one line plus its Rust arm |
| Why a Rust command | The decoding and the cue parsing live in Rust, so the text cannot be derived from the bytes the webview already holds. Two thin commands, one per root; the text is read when the mode is chosen (one file, on a click) |
| Mail bytes versus subtitle text | A mail is handed to its reader as stored: the reader decodes the declared character set, and bytes already turned into UTF-8 would be decoded twice (an 8-bit message in ISO-8859-1 would show `Ã©`). Subtitles take the raw view's conversion, which also reads UTF-16, something the indexing path does not do (it would read a UTF-16 subtitle file as Windows-1252, a pre-existing gap, not touched) |
| Error code | No new code: a format without a readable text is refused as `viewer_file_unreadable` (the webview never asks for one) |

## Taken while building the viewer tabs (17 October 2026)

Part 04 of the workspace series (`feat/document-viewer`). Behaviour: `docs/CLIENT.md`, "The document viewer", "Tabs".

| Subject | Decision |
| --- | --- |
| Only the active tab is mounted | No other viewer, canvas or PDFium instance exists. Coming back to a tab **reads the file again** (no byte cache: a file can be 128 MiB). Chosen for libraries of 1 500+ files |
| Identity | Folder + relative path (`tabId`, NUL-joined), so the same name in both folders gives two tabs. Opening an open file activates it; a different hash replaces the record and drops the remembered view |
| One way to open | `openFile(folder, record)` in `App`, which calls `openTab`. A cap on the number of tabs would be a change in `openTab` only. **The cap is not decided** (open) |
| Keys | `tablist` pattern with automatic activation (moving focus activates, since switching is cheap). Left / Right wrap, Home / End. Roving tabindex: only the active tab and its close button are in the Tab order. No shortcut (Ctrl+W...), no reordering, no pinning (open, register item 9) |
| Closing | The active tab hands over to its right neighbour, else the left one. The last one collapses the centre. **Fermer** left the viewer header (it would have been a second, ambiguous close) |
| What is remembered | In memory only, never across launches (open, register item 3): scroll as a 0 to 1 ratio, zoom only if the user chose one, sheet, slide (by stepping), Markdown mode. Saved when the tab is left or closed, only if the view had finished restoring, so React's double effect in development cannot overwrite the real state. Reading the store does not consume the entry, for the same reason |
| Keeping a few viewers alive | Not done (open, register item 2) |
| Restore | Frame by frame until the scrollable height has been stable for 3 frames, 2 s at most, abandoned at the first wheel, key or pointer input. The scrolling element is found from the browser's own report (overflow and computed style), never by a class of the library |
| Outside change | One check at window `focus` for **all** open tabs: at most one inventory read per folder that has a tab, the tabs that differ close together and one modal covers the batch. The others stay. A tab whose folder could not be read is left alone. A folder replaced in the settings closes its tabs silently |
| Title-bar button | Owner, 17 October: it **hides** the workspace without closing tabs or their views, and shows it again; so does dragging the centre closed. Opening a file shows it. Hidden, the viewers are unmounted (a collapsed zone is 0 px wide, which would refit a document to nothing) and each tab is restored from its remembered view |
| Page counter | The library draws a DOCX as a flow with fewer `.dv-page` elements than pages (133 reported), so the counter stuck at 1. It reads the elements only when there is one per page, otherwise it estimates from the scroll position (`pageFromScroll`, exact for equal pages, close otherwise). Confirmed by the owner on a 133-page DOCX and on an ODT saved from it with LibreOffice (17 October) |

## Speech: workstation-local dictation, read-aloud and voice notes (17 October 2026)

Owner decisions of 17 October 2026, completed on 18 October 2026 (branch layout, French voices, hardware, Files / Notes switch, hosting
format). They end the hold on the voice track (KB D7, D8 and "No longer frozen" above): the programme is resumed. Canonical spec:
`docs/SPEECH.md`. Programme rule: `.cursor/rules/speech-programme.mdc`. Reference, read only: the owner's repository `dekpo/SpeechLab`; its
integration plan v1 is a superseded draft (its ON HOLD banner, D-050, no longer applies).

| Subject | Decision |
| --- | --- |
| D1 — base branch | `feat/speech-integration`, created by the owner from `feat/document-viewer`, which stays untouched (safety branch `backup/before-speech`). One branch per phase, `feat/speech-<topic>`, merged back by pull request. Layout facts used: three resizable zones (resources, workspace, chat), the `WorkspaceView` union in `lib/workspaceView.ts`, a serialised settings save |
| D2 — speech to text | NVIDIA Parakeet TDT 0.6B v3 int8 (CC BY 4.0, attribution required) through sherpa-onnx, in a **separate worker executable**. Nothing speech-related is linked into the main executable. whisper.cpp (MIT) may be added later behind the same trait and is the recorded fallback. **This is the "explicit later decision" that KB D7 reserved for a CC-BY model**; the free-licence rule (`docs/MODELS.md`) stays for every other model |
| D3 — text to speech | Two engines behind one interface: Piper through a **separate TTS worker** (preferred) and operating-system voices as a fallback **only if proven local**. French: Piper `siwis` (female) and Coqui `css10` (male; the owner confirms by ear in phase 9, SpeechLab recorded no gender) for the controlled prototype; English: `ljspeech` or `libritts_r`. `mls` is rejected (heard by the owner), `gilles` is excluded. Voice licences are verified before any distribution. **`siwis` is rated `review`** (fine-tuned from the Lessac voice, whose data is a research-only agreement): accepted for the controlled prototype only, because the pilot is the owner's relative; its licence is settled, and it is retrained from the clean base checkpoint or cleared in writing, before **any** wider distribution (a later track, outside the 16 phases). The rating stays visible in the voice list and in `docs/SPEECH.md`. The named voices are the explicit decision for their licences (BSD-3-Clause, CC BY 4.0, `review`): the free-licence rule of `docs/MODELS.md` governs the chat model families |
| D4 — nothing leaves | No audio, transcript, answer text or name ever leaves the machine through the speech module. "Local" is proven by an offline test: a firewall rule blocking the application and both workers, no connection opened |
| D5 — model download | Models are downloaded on an **explicit user action** from `https://assistantcabinet.ai/models/speech/` (the owner's host; the URL is a setting, `speechModelsUrl`, and the path may be corrected by the owner), SHA-256 pinned in the application, hosted as `.zip` (decided 18 October). It is the **only network code of the speech module, in one named file**. Side-loading files by hand is the second path (offline practice machine, antivirus TLS interception) |
| D6 — languages | fr-FR and en-US now. German and Italian (the Swiss target) are **anticipated**: a data-driven language table (interface locale, engine language code, pack names, capability flags, default voices), locale packs for rules and voice commands, no `if fr / else en` anywhere, an unsupported locale answers `speech_locale_unsupported`. Dictation language follows the interface locale, with an override setting |
| D7 — names | After a dictation, "heard to display" suggestions are shown: selection only, the hidden-names setting honoured, nothing new revealed. Never a silent replacement, never a write to the KB. Generic over entity types and subtypes (a future medication entity needs no change; no drug list ships). **Three guards**, all required: the names are those of the current selection; the hidden-names setting collapses them; a chip is a suggestion the user accepts or ignores |
| D8 — read-aloud | An explicit button, local only. It reads the answer text, the engine-computed block and the caution notes (including the two lines saying which side of a mixed question could not be established); never timings, sources or coverage. Play and Stop only. **No auto-read, no spoken conversation mode** |
| D9 — notes | A button in the title bar beside Settings (own switch `notesEnabled`, default on, independent of the voice master switch); a **Notes** section of the centre zone reached by a **Files / Notes** switch at its top (decided 18 October: the file tabs are kept, both sections stay mounted). Typeless-like pane: the text fills it with Markdown rendered (Raw / Preview), a dark pill button centred at the bottom (microphone at rest, waveform from the input level while recording, "click again to finish"), the save block under the text. Saved in `<documents folder>/Notes`; file name = local timestamp + the user's name field, sanitised, auto-numbered, never overwritten; default `.md` (`.txt` option); UTF-8 without BOM. Voice commands: punctuation, title, subtitle, new line, new paragraph, new note; a "title" runs to the end of the sentence, a "new line" command or a pause (about 0.7 s). **Save also analyses** the saved note (the click is the user's act; nothing else starts an Analyse). Analysing only the saved files is impossible without touching the indexing code, so Save runs the **full Analyse pass**, with a confirmation when other files are also pending (validated by the owner). A confirmation modal waits for the user before an unsaved note can be lost; one dictation is capped at 30 minutes (setting) |
| D10 — no recovery copy | No recovery copy of an unsaved note is written to disk |
| D11 — macOS | Maximise compatibility now: no `#[cfg]` outside platform modules, sidecars named per target triple, capture behind an interface so that a native fallback can replace `getUserMedia`. A Mac is available in November (phase 14) |
| D12 — test machine | The owner's laptop (Core 7 150U, 24 GB, Windows 11); SpeechLab's figures apply. The interface never freezes: all engine work is out of process. The practice workstation is being replaced, so the same short check runs on the new computer when it exists (`docs/SPEECH.md`, "Minimum configuration"), with whisper.cpp as the fallback engine |
| D13 — non-regression | With the voice master switch off, the Composer, the answer action row and every other existing component render exactly as before (baseline render tests written before the first edit of phase 2). The Notes button and section are the only visible change when `notesEnabled` is on, and they are governed by their own switch |
| D14 — a note is a document | A saved note is an ordinary document (validated by the owner): once saved and analysed, its text follows the same path as every document (embedding through the practice server on the local network, questions to the model). "Audio and transcripts never cross the network" applies to audio, and to dictated text until the user saves it |

**Voice rules, restated.** Read-aloud speaks text already on screen, with local engines only. Dictation fills a text box and does nothing
else. `/v1/audio/*` stays reserved and unused. A voice never confirms or applies a file action: saving a note is the user's click on a
preview of the exact files. Cloud speech stays forbidden.

**Invariants, each proved by a named test (`docs/SPEECH.md`, section 12).** Audio is never kept. No network from the module except the model
download file. A transcript only fills a text box. Voice never applies a file action. Dictation never writes the KB. No text in logs.
Only proven-local voices. Spoken text is derived by code, never by the model. Any failure degrades to the typed application. Cancel works at
every stage. One speech job at a time. Workers die with the app. Nothing heavy starts at launch.

**Decided on 18 October 2026, with the re-baseline.** French voices as in D3. Hardware as in D12. Hosting as `.zip`. The Files / Notes switch
of D9. Read-aloud reads the two "which side could not be established" lines of a mixed question (not their `not_asked_about` variants, not
`documentsNotNeeded`). The Parakeet CC BY 4.0 attribution text and the licence rows are in `docs/SPEECH.md`, "Licences".

**D3 amended on 18 October 2026, after the phase 8 human test (owner decision).** Operating-system voices are proven local on the owner's laptop (Windows 11, WebView2): with the network switched off the reading is unchanged, and the system lists three local French voices (Microsoft Hortense, Julie and Paul). The owner decides that **system voices stay a permanent, supported option** next to Piper, not a stop-gap: they give users more choice and need no model download. They remain opt-in (engine setting "System voices"); "Automatic" does not fall back to them, because the webview's voices cannot be probed from Rust and an online one would send text away (`localService === false` voices are never used). The voice heard in the test was female (Hortense); a male system voice (Paul) is installed but **cannot be chosen yet**, see `docs/SPEECH.md`, open point 11 (**built in phase 9 on 19 October 2026**: the setting `ttsSystemVoice` and a voice list that shows the system's local voices when the engine is `system`). Whether "Automatic" should offer system voices when Piper is absent is not decided.

**Points settled on 18 October 2026** (`docs/SPEECH.md`, "Open points"): the owner confirmed that D3 settles the voice licences against the
free-licence policy and accepted the recommended default of every other point. Still waiting for a measurement or her ear, not a decision: the
gender of css10 and the new workstation's measurements. The points settled by default are: the licences of the voices, the licence of the application's own code and the credits view, the GPL-3.0 position of espeak-ng inside a separate TTS
executable, the exact hosting path, the form of the baseline tests, a Markdown crate for read-aloud, a CI step for the worker crate, the KB
spoken forms in read-aloud, the gender of css10, and the new workstation's measurements.

**Where the helper programs and the models live (owner decision, 19 October 2026).** Models stay online and are downloaded on an explicit user action (D5): they are heavy and carry no code. The helper programs of the application (the dictation worker, the voice worker and the Tesseract sidecar) are **bundled in the installer**, not downloaded: they are small (the voice worker is 19 MB), an executable fetched from the Internet would be the only code of the product to arrive that way, an antivirus or SmartScreen treats a program downloaded after the install less kindly than one it came with, and a practice workstation without Internet at install time must still work. One mechanism for the three programs, Windows and macOS, built by a script and a CI step; the voice worker travels with its own espeak-ng notice. Consequence: the rule "the only network code of the speech module is the model download file" is kept as it is. Phase 13 of the speech programme wires this; the 14 October milestone stays withdrawn as a date, and the installer track stays gated by item 6 of `AGENTS.md`.

**How it is wired (phase 13, 19 October 2026; owner choice of the separate file).** The list of bundled programs and files is
`apps/desktop/src-tauri/tauri.bundle.conf.json` (`bundle.externalBin` and `bundle.resources`), the one place that says which programs
go in, and `scripts/build-installer.sh` builds and stages what it names and runs `tauri build --config` with it. The entries are **not**
in `tauri.conf.json`: Tauri checks every `externalBin` and `resources` file at every build, test runs included, so declared there they
would stop `cargo test` and `tauri dev` on any machine that has not staged the programs (measured in SpeechLab, I-064, and in this
repository's `binaries/README.md` history). The wording "in `tauri.conf.json`" of the decision above is read as "in the build
configuration". A test (`speech/packaging_tests.rs`) pins the three names, the notice, the places the finders look and the fact that the
default configuration names none. Windows entries only: the macOS ones wait for the macOS phase and for a Tesseract fetch script for macOS.

## Settled by the owner: the left panel becomes one file explorer (18 October 2026)

Design review of the left panel (the two folder cards), from the audit of the code and the interactive mock-up of 18 October 2026. Nothing is built yet: this records what the owner validated. Points still open are listed at the end.

| Subject | Decision |
| --- | --- |
| Direction | **One box, two families.** The Documents Folder and the Data Folder stay two physical folders with two analysis pipelines; the explorer is an **interface layer above them**. Two folder icons of a different look (documents, tables) head two sections of one box, each with its own **Analyser (n)** button. The two families stay visibly distinct because the product treats them by two different methods. Reliability of the document and tabular pipelines is never traded for a simpler screen |
| The search no longer selects (replaces owner D4 of 14 October 2026) | A search, like every filter, **only changes what the list shows**. Selecting files for the conversation is a separate click: **« Cocher les N résultats »**, offered under the list, which says how many results cannot be ticked because they are not analysed. Clearing the field never changes the selection (as before). **Why the change:** (1) the selection decides which files an answer may rest on, so it must only change by a visible act of the user, and a text field is not one; (2) a search that finds nothing, or an extension or date typed as a word, silently emptied the selection, which looked like a bug and gave « aucun » to the next question; (3) it contradicted the finder's own rule that a result is a list and never changes the selection (`docs/LEXICAL-RETRIEVAL-DESIGN.md`, section 6); (4) filtering (a view) and selecting (an effect) were one gesture, which made a filter by type, state or knowledge-base entity impossible to add without more surprises. A test pins that typing, clearing and filtering never change the selection. **Optional « Select. auto » switch (owner proposal, 18 October 2026, agreed):** a switch in the upper part of the box makes the selection follow the filtered results of analysed files, for users who want speed. Turning it on is the visible act that replaces the typing. Safeguards **validated by the owner on 18 October 2026**: off by default; a manual tick turns it off (so a later filter never erases a hand-made choice); a one-step « restore the previous selection »; its state is always shown in the selection bar; it is locked while a question is being answered, like the selection itself |
| Two Analyser buttons | **One per family**, each with its count of files still to analyse. A **« Tout analyser »** that runs both in sequence is a later addition, never a replacement. The heavy work still runs only inside an Analyse the user started by hand |
| Filters | In one click, combinable: family, format (only the formats present, with counts), state (analysed, to analyse, unreadable, error); then the knowledge-base filters (person, organisation, business type, year), which use the finder's existing `formats`, `states`, `kinds`, `entities` and `date` fields and facets. Typing `.xlsx` or `xlsx` must act as the format filter. The finder is not changed for this beyond what the audit lists |
| Flat list | The list under each folder shows **name and extension, never the path** (the start of a long name is cut, never its extension; the full path is on hover and under « Voir »). Collections (« Vues ») are saved filters: they never copy, move or rename a file |
| Direction for files in the other folder | A spreadsheet in the Documents Folder is shown under Tables, a document in the Data Folder under Documents, **by extension**, with a small tag giving the folder it sits in. Nothing moves on the disk |
| Two buttons and misplaced files | Two buttons, one per family: validated. A file of a family sitting in the other physical folder is **analysed by its own family's button, in place**: the tabular pass also reads the spreadsheets found in the Documents Folder, the document pass also reads the documents found in the Data Folder. **Read only** (the file is never copied, moved or modified by the reading) and the **folder it sits in is remembered** with the file, so it can be opened, shown in the file manager and told apart from a file of the same name in the other folder (owner validated, 18 October 2026). Before the button is pressed the file stays « to analyse » in its own family's section, and that family's button counts it |
| Origin folder filter | Validated (18 October 2026): a **« Dossier d'origine »** filter lists the user's sub-folders with their file counts; picking one shows only its files, documents and tables staying in their two sections; nothing moves. When two files share a name, the sub-folder is shown in grey beside the name; the full path stays on hover |
| File names: documents are cleaned, spreadsheets are not | **Verified in the code on 18 October 2026: spreadsheets are never renamed today.** `filename_sanitizer::sanitize_folder` walks `discovery::discover`, which keeps only the document extensions (`SUPPORTED_EXTENSIONS` excludes `csv`, `xlsx`, `xls`, `ods`); the Data pass calls the same function (`commands.rs`, `index_data_folder`), so it renames a document found in the Data Folder and never a workbook. **Owner decision, 18 October 2026: this stays.** Documents are renamed to clean names so that the AI models and the matching do not fail on spaces, accents or two Unicode spellings; **spreadsheets are never touched**, because they are expected to carry coherent names already: other programs produce and consume them, and workbooks can refer to one another by name, so a rename could break an export, a link or a process outside the product. The rule is by **family, wherever the file sits**: the document pass renames a document found in the Data Folder as it does in the Documents Folder, with the same log; no pass ever renames a workbook. Rejected options: renaming workbooks too (an unnoticed second copy after a recurring export, figures counted twice, broken links between workbooks), and renaming them except when they reappear. `AGENTS.md` rule 3 stays as written. To fix in the code: the comment of `index_data_folder` says « the same rename », which is true only for documents |

No question is open on these points. Findings of the audit that the work must settle (search of dates and extensions by name, stale results, the rename on Analyse, the counts that differ between the two cards, the selection lost by a rename, the missing Stop on the tabular pass) are listed in the work plan, not decided here.

## Settled by the owner: the explorer's layout, first start, progress and centre (18 October 2026, second review)

Second design review, on the interactive mock-up of the five states. Nothing is built yet. Where a mechanism is the agent's proposal and not yet confirmed, it says **to confirm**.

| Subject | Decision |
| --- | --- |
| Layout of the box | **Compact by default.** Each family has **its own file list with its own scroll bar** (a single shared scroll was refused: too many nested bars with 1 000+ files). Each family can be **folded and unfolded** with a small button to the right of the « ⋯ » menu; with both folded the box is only its header, which leaves room for the other boxes planned below it. Height budget shared by the open lists, **to confirm**: two open families get six rows each, one open family gets twelve, so the box never grows with the number of files |
| Density | Compact by default; a **Comfortable** option lives in **Settings**, not as a control in the box |
| Filter panel | Opens as a panel with **no scroll bar**. **To confirm:** format and state are pills (few values), the origin folder and the people or organisations are typed fields that offer at most five suggestions and say « N autres : tapez pour affiner » |
| Header | Search field and « Filtres » button on one row; the segmented control **Tout / Documents / Tableaux** with counts; compact counters in each family header (● ○ ▲); active filters as removable pills |
| Folder menu | The « ⋯ » menu per family (**Voir le dossier**, **Changer de dossier**, **Effacer l'analyse**) is validated. « Reset » is renamed **« Effacer l'analyse »**: short, understandable, and a key in both catalogues |
| Bottom bar | **Light, like the rest of the box, separated by a border like the header**, as short as possible: one line (what is used, a few small links, the « Select. auto » switch), a second line only when there is something to say, short sentences, no large buttons |
| First start | « Pour commencer » with three steps worded **« Choisissez deux dossiers. », « Déposez-y vos fichiers. », « Appuyez sur Analyser. »**, and the button **« Créer les deux dossiers suggérés »** (validated). Per family: the type line **« Documents : PDF, DOCX, ODT… »** and **« Tableaux : CSV, XLSX, ODS… »**, the suggested path, and the two buttons **side by side**, **« Créer et utiliser »** and **« Choisir un dossier »** (no trailing dots). The long explanations about OneDrive and iCloud are removed from the box (they stay on hover of the path). **No scroll bar in the box at start-up**, whatever the window |
| Folders created, still empty | Validated: one line per family (« Vide. Déposez-y vos fichiers. », **Voir le dossier**, **Changer**), no blank space under the box; same type lines as above, for consistency |
| Progress of an Analyse | **The Analyser button becomes the gauge** (percentage in its label, spinner, fill) with a stop button; two named stages for documents (**reading, then meaning search**, each with its percentage); a line with files read, time elapsed and time remaining; the remaining time shown only after 10 % and 8 s, as « environ », otherwise « estimation en cours… », computed from the recent rate and not the overall average. **No « ⋯ » menu on a family while it is being analysed** (nothing in it can be done). The thin 2 px line under the header is **to be tested** (a switch on the mock-up compares). **Stop is available for both families** (the tabular pass has none today, and the single stop flag is shared by both passes: the explorer work gives each family its own). Tables: a pass over about fifty workbooks can take a minute and a half, so the tabular pass shows the same gauge; a plain spinner stays in reserve if it proves noisy (**to confirm**). End of pass: « Terminé · 1 245 analysés, 3 illisibles · 6 min 49 s » and, **on its own line**, « Voir le rapport » |
| Reports in the centre | The analysis reports leave the sidebar and are shown in the centre zone; the centre is **never opened by force**. The owner asked to check the default state of the centre and to propose; see the verified facts and the proposal below |
| Minimum widths | The owner asks that the left zone can never be narrow enough to wrap its content (a wrapped line makes the box taller), for **every state of the application**. See the verified facts and the proposal below |

Verified in the code on 18 October 2026:

- **Left zone width.** `AppLayout.tsx`: `RESOURCES_MIN_PX = 240`, default 22 %, maximum 35 %; `panelLayout.ts`: defaults `[22, 38, 40]` (centre open) and `[26, 0, 74]` (centre closed); the window is 1 180 x 780 with a **minimum of 1 000 x 620** (`tauri.conf.json`). At the minimum window the left zone is 240 px (closed centre: 260 px), narrower than the explorer needs (the mock-up is designed for 320 px).
- **Centre.** At launch the centre is closed (two zones). The button of the three panels opens it on `{ kind: "welcome" }`, whose only content is the title « Espace de travail » and a paragraph announcing that the zone « will welcome » documents. File tabs exist only in the `files` view (`TabStrip`).
- **Audio branch.** The audio work, merged into the default branch since this review was written, adds, behind the setting `speech.notesEnabled`, a **`Files | Notes` switch at the top of the centre** (`CentreSwitch`, `lib/centreSection.ts`), a Notes button in the title bar, and a Notes panel kept mounted beside the files. `WorkspaceView` itself is unchanged (`welcome` or `files`). Saving a note writes a file into the Documents folder and starts the application's own Analyse (`indexing.run()`), and `WorkFolderCard` gets a `refreshToken` prop for it. The notes are therefore **sections of the centre, not tabs of the file strip**.

Proposals, **to confirm**:

1. **A third centre section, « Aperçu » (overview), first of the switch: `Aperçu | Fichiers | Notes`.** It replaces the welcome text and holds, in one page, the state of the workspace and the report of the last analysis. Before the first analysis it says what is ready and what is left to do; afterwards it gives the figures per family, the files to check (with their reason and a link that filters the left list), the cleaned names in full, the unread formats, the knowledge base and where the time went. The line « Voir le rapport » under each family leads there. It is one page that updates, not one tab per report. The switch appears whenever the centre is open (today it exists only when Notes is enabled); `Notes` stays hidden unless enabled. A centre opened by its button with no file open shows Aperçu.
2. **Left zone minimum 320 px** (`RESOURCES_MIN_PX`), defaults `[30, 0, 70]` and `[28, 34, 38]`, window minimum unchanged at 1 000 px (320 + 320 + 340 = 980). Every label of the box is on one line with an ellipsis (`nowrap`), except the sentences designed to wrap; a visual check at 320 px in every state is part of each lot's human test.
3. **Base of the work:** the explorer work starts from the current default-branch code, which already holds the document viewer and the audio work. Files the explorer and the centre both touch (`App.tsx`, `AppLayout.tsx`, `TitleBar.tsx`, `WorkFolderCard.tsx`, `SettingsDialog.tsx`, `useAppSettings.ts`, `ipc.ts`, `styles.css`, both locale catalogues) are the conflict zone.

## Taken while building the explorer's search step (19 October 2026), to confirm

Step 1 of the left-panel explorer (`docs/SESSION-EXPLORER/EXPLORER-01-search.md`). Built, to confirm with the owner when she has played the human test.

| Subject | Taken |
| --- | --- |
| The search no longer selects | `useFinderSearch` has no selection callback and the cards no longer call `scopeFromSearch` (removed). Under the result line, **« Cocher les N résultats »** adds the analysed results that are not yet selected with `addScopeFiles` (N counts only those; hidden at 0; a selection that is already « tous » is left as it is). The line also says how many results are not analysed and cannot be ticked. Clearing the field, « Réinitialiser » and « Tous les fichiers » never touch the selection (the state module holds no selection, a guard test reads the sources) |
| Extension typed as a word | In the `find_files` command (`finder/typed.rs`, pure): a typed word that is a file extension leaves the words and becomes a **format filter**. **With a leading dot it always is one** (`.docx`, `.xyz`; it needs a letter, at most ten letters and digits). **Without a dot, only if the product reads that extension** (`FileKind::from_extension`: pdf, docx, xlsx, csv, txt, md, odt, ods, jpg...) **or the folder holds a file of it**. Capitals do not matter. `rapport .docx` is the word `rapport` among the docx; `.docx` alone lists every docx (the finder already returned filter-only queries). Typed extensions are added to the formats a caller already asked for. **Consequence, accepted:** `pdf` can no longer be used to find documents whose *text* mentions « pdf » (it was the defect: the Documents card matched passages), and `fichier pdf` means the pdf files whose name has `fichier`. A whole name typed with its extension (`rapport.docx`) is still a name. The chat's set-sentence road is unchanged |
| Dates typed | A text that is **only** a date also asks for the files dated so: `YYYY` (1990 to 2100), `YYYY-MM`, `YYYY-MM-DD`, `DD/MM/YYYY`, `DD-MM-YYYY` (the two French forms fold to the ISO day; a month or a day is checked as a calendar; `2026/02/06`, `06.02.2026`, `2026-2` are only words). A file is dated so by the date in its **profile** or by the **clean date its name starts with**, analysed or not (`2026-02-06_commandes.csv` is found before and after an Analyse). The typed words are **kept** and searched as before (names, profile; the passages are **not** searched for a date), so `2026` still finds `facture-fac-2026-015` by its identifier words, and `FAC-2026-014` typed in full is not a date. The hit says `date` with the detail `profile` or `name`. A workbook's date-column span (used by the `date` range filter) does not count for a typed date. The `date` range filter itself now also reads the name's date for a file with no profile date (it excluded every file never analysed) |
| Freshness | The state module `lib/finderSearchState.ts` (reducer, tested) asks for a new search at once when a pass finishes or the inventory is read again (window focus included), keeps the results on screen until the answer arrives, clears the text and the results when the folder changes, and drops every answer that is not the latest request (typing again, clearing, a folder change, the field being disabled) |
| Small ones | Punctuation alone (`!!`, `.`, `...`) is left as typed and finds nothing (it does not list the whole folder). One letter is a whole word and never the start of one (two letters are a start). `œ`, `æ` and `ß` are expanded in the name words (`words_of`), so `cœur` meets `coeur` and `straße` meets `strasse`; NFD input already worked and is pinned by a test |

## Taken while building the explorer's unified view (19 October 2026), to confirm

Step 2 of the left-panel explorer (`docs/SESSION-EXPLORER/EXPLORER-02-view.md`). Built, to confirm with the owner when she has played the human test.

| Subject | Taken |
| --- | --- |
| Where the rules live | `lib/explorerModel.ts` (families, states, merge, filters, sort, counts, duplicate names), `lib/explorerWindow.ts` (row height, height budget, window arithmetic), `lib/explorerSelection.ts` (what « Cocher les N », « Décocher » and the second line of the bottom bar do), all pure and tested. The components only draw them. No new inventory command, no change to the pipelines, the scopes or the chat contract |
| A file of the other family | Always shown as **to analyse**, whatever its own folder says (a document of the Data folder is forced to `failed` there, a workbook of the Documents folder is « not assessed »: neither describes its own pipeline). It cannot be ticked, opened or revealed (steps 8 and 9) and is not counted by its family's **Analyser (n)**, whose tooltip says so; the header's « to analyse » counter does include it |
| Files no pipeline reads | Set apart, **not listed**, said once on the second line of the bottom bar (« N fichiers d'un format non lu ne sont pas listés »). Not in the handoff: added so that a file cannot vanish without a word (her Documents folder holds one `.ppt`) |
| Overlapping folders | The merge, given both folder paths, keeps one entry per physical file (native copy first, then the Documents one). Without the paths nothing is merged |
| Both families stay visible whatever the segmented control says | As in the mock-up: the family that is filtered out shows « Aucun fichier ne correspond. ». The segment's counts are those of the search results |
| What the box dropped from the cards | « Tous les fichiers » / « Résultats seulement » and « Réinitialiser » (the field is cleared by emptying it, and the mock-up has no such controls); the « Documents utilisés » disclosure and its select-all box (replaced by the bottom bar). The search status line (« N fichiers trouvés pour … », « Dont non cochables ») stays under the segmented control |
| « Cocher les N » | Shown when the view is narrowed by anything (the search, the family, later the filters), and adds the **analysed files of their own folder that the view shows and that are not yet ticked**, in both folders, through `addScopeFiles`. « Décocher » empties both sides |
| Second line of the bottom bar | Only when there is something to say: nothing used on either side (one short sentence), a **whole family** used (one sentence per family: it widens silently), files of unread formats. Not per family for « aucun »: with nothing used on either side one sentence says it |
| Analyse text kept | The lines the cards showed after a pass (failed files with reasons, renames, unread formats, knowledge-base counts) moved to `lib/passSummary.ts` and are shown in a **« Dernière analyse »** disclosure under each family header (open by itself when a file failed), so that nothing is lost between this step and the report in the centre (step 6). The two old cards call the same functions (their words are unchanged). The profile « the index must be rebuilt » note and the meaning-search share note are kept as small notes under the header, and the pass error banners at the bottom of the box |
| Folder actions until step 4 | The box has no « Voir / Changer / Reset »: choosing, changing and clearing a folder live in the Settings panel, whose two cards are untouched. A family with no folder says so and points to the Settings. The guarded change of the Documents folder (unsaved notes ask first) moved from the sidebar card to `chooseWorkFolder` in `App.tsx` and is now also given to the Settings panel's card (before, only the sidebar card had it) |
| Settings keys | `explorer { density, documentsFolded, tablesFolded }`, read **leniently** in Rust (each field falls back to its default, a block of any shape reads as the defaults, the file never becomes unreadable), written through the settings queue (`saveExplorer`). Density is chosen in the Settings panel only |
| Inventory cost | Measured on 1 500 files of 100 KB per folder (generated outside the repository, deleted), **debug build** (`cargo test`), no index, a fresh hash cache each time: `work_folder_inventory` 6.2 s then 4.7 s, `data_folder_inventory` 5.2 s then 4.7 s; with the hash cache already filled (a window focus after the first read) both together 0.55 s, so about 0.27 s each. All of it over the 200 ms line, and a debug build is what `tauri dev` runs; a release build is expected to be much lower and was not measured. `work_folder_inventory` and `data_folder_inventory` are now `#[tauri::command(async)]`: a first read hashes every file, and the box reads both at launch and at every return of the window focus |
| Keyboard | The scroll area of a list that scrolls takes the focus, so the arrow keys scroll it; only the rows drawn (the ones in view plus four each side) are in the tab order |
| Left zone minimum (owner's test, 20 October 2026) | A family header (folder icon, name and counters, Analyser, fold) **never wraps**: the left zone's minimum is **320 px** (was 240) and the window's minimum **1 080 px** (was 1 000). The name and the counters are the part that gives way (ellipsis). This brings forward the width rule of step 4 |
| Density select (same test) | Fixed: the handler read the select's value after the settings queue had run, so nothing was saved. Folds and density were checked on the real application (relaunched three times, settings read back) |

## Taken while building the explorer's filter panel (20 October 2026), to confirm

Step 3 of the left-panel explorer (`docs/SESSION-EXPLORER/EXPLORER-03-filter-panel.md`). Built, to confirm with the owner when she has played the human test.

| Subject | Taken |
| --- | --- |
| Where the rules live | `ExplorerFilter` (with the new `folders`) and `filterEntries` stay in `lib/explorerModel.ts`; the panel's own rules (counts, suggestions, pills, reasons) are in the new `lib/explorerFilters.ts`, pure and tested, so the model file does not grow past its job. One filter type, no second one |
| Counting rule | The number beside a choice, and the segmented control's, is counted over what **all the other filters** leave, with the choice's own kind left out (`leftByOthers`). Choices of one kind combine as **or** (pdf or docx), kinds combine as **and**. A choice that would empty the list shows 0 (dimmed, still clickable) |
| A typed extension narrows | `.docx` typed beside a `pdf` pill shows nothing: the search finds the docx files, the pill then picks among them, and the `pdf` pill says 0 before it is ticked. It never widens to « pdf or docx ». The alternative (widening) would need the pills sent to the finder and every count re-asked on each click; not worth it. The lit pill comes from the answer's echo `FinderResult.typedFormats` (a small Rust change: `apply_typed_text` returns the extensions it took out, `find_files` sets the field; a test pins that the formats a caller already asked for are not echoed). Pressing a lit typed pill removes the word from the text (`withoutTypedFormat`: it only drops the words the finder already called extensions) |
| Origin folder | A folder is identified by **root and folder** (`documents:clients/2025`), because the two roots can hold the same name; the choice says its root (« 2025 (Docs) ») only when both do. A chosen folder keeps its files **and everything under it**; every folder and each ancestor is offered, with the files in it or under it among what the other filters leave (so `patients` and `patients/ordonnances` can show the same number). A file at the top of its root is in no folder. Several folders may be chosen (or). Suggestions: at most **five**, most files first, only while the field has the focus, accents and case ignored, folders already chosen left out, then « N autres : tapez pour affiner » |
| The panel is in the flow | Not a pop-over: the box clips its overflow, and nothing may sit below it. It opens under the search line and pushes the sections down; the lists' height budget is unchanged. No scroll area: the format pills wrap |
| Closing | Escape and « Terminé » give the focus back to the « Filtres » button; a click outside closes it and leaves the focus where the click put it. Escape in a `type="search"` field also clears that field in the webview (native behaviour, left alone) |
| « Filtres » button | Disabled only while **no folder at all** is chosen (the panel's components take a `disabled` flag for step 4's first-start drawing). Badge = the number of pills in force. The « Vues » switch keeps its `explorer.soon` title |
| Reasons of the hits | The paths-only request **already returns the reasons** (`FileHit.reasons` is filled whatever `pathsOnly` says; `pathsOnly` only drops cards' entities, excerpts and locators), so no request changes. The reducer keeps `reasons` by path and `typedFormats` beside `found`. A row shows the **first** reason in its fixed slot (grey, ellipsis, same rule as the folder hint) and all of them in its tooltip; the order is name, title, folder, content, cells, headers, number, entities, dates, subject, author, card. `folder` (a word met the folder's name) is a reason the finder reports and the handoff's list did not name: « dans le nom du dossier ». Row height unchanged |
| Cost of the reasons | Measured 20 October 2026 on a **copy of her analysed index** (1 252 files in `AssistantCabinetAI-test/Docs`, 17 in her Data folder), debug build: a typed word answered in 14 to 207 ms, the widest (`2026`, 1 242 hits) 207 ms and 562 KB of JSON of which the reasons are 151 KB (27 %); the pure work of one render of the explorer (filters, counts, suggestions, pills) 2.3 ms for 1 268 entries and 1 242 hits. Accepted: nothing is added to the request |

## Taken while building the explorer's analysis gauge (20 October 2026), to confirm

Step 5 of the left-panel explorer (`docs/SESSION-EXPLORER/EXPLORER-05-progress.md`). Built; the owner's human test passed on 20 October 2026 (last rows).

| Subject | Taken |
| --- | --- |
| One Stop flag per folder | `AppState.analysis_stop` is now `AnalysisStops` (`analysis_stop.rs`), one atomic per `Domain`. `cancel_analysis(domain)` raises that folder's flag; each pass lowers only its own at start. A Stop on one family leaves the other running, and starting a pass no longer cancels a Stop pressed on the other. TypeScript `cancelAnalysis(domain)` (`"documents"` or `"data"`; the Tables family is `data`); the old Documents card passes `documents` |
| The tabular pass is asynchronous and stoppable | `index_data_folder` is an `async` command; the whole pass runs in `spawn_blocking` (`run_data_pass`), so the window keeps drawing. The loop of `data_folder::analyse_cancellable` reads the flag **between two workbooks**; `analyse` and `analyse_with_knowledge` keep their signatures (they pass `\|\| false`). The summary says `cancelled` (the field already existed) |
| What a stopped tabular pass leaves | Every workbook read before the stop is analysed and stays so. Each other workbook keeps the inventory an earlier pass gave it: green if its content is unchanged, back to « to analyse » if it changed or was never read. The typed cell cache, cleared up front exactly as in a complete pass, is only a cache (a question re-reads the file on a miss), so a partial pass leaves nothing inconsistent. A stopped pass **skips what follows the loop** (forgetting vanished workbooks, re-reading documents for the new names, the file profiles): the next pass does it, as the Documents pass already does. `timings.files_processed` counts the workbooks actually read. A complete pass is unchanged (a test compares it with the plain `analyse`) |
| Estimator | `lib/analysisProgress.ts`, pure. Stages: reading, names (still stage 1 of 2), meaning (stage 2 of 2); the tables have one. Percentage of the stage floored, never above 100, with the batches inside a long file. Remaining time: shown only once the stage is at 10 % **and** has run 8 s (the stage's own clock, not the pass's); rate over the last **15 s** of messages (at least two), the estimate of the finishing moment **smoothed with 0.3**; no estimate while nothing moved over the window; a stage that starts over forgets everything. Messages closer than 250 ms are merged, so the series stays small. These values are proposals: to confirm after the owner's run |
| The remaining time is the stage's | During reading, the length of the meaning stage is not known, so « restantes » is the time left in the stage being run, not in the pass. Said here so nobody reads it as a promise |
| Rounding | Under 60 s: « moins d'une minute restante »; then « environ N min restantes » (« environ 1 min restante »); from 90 min, « environ N h restantes ». Elapsed: « 3 min 48 s » |
| End of pass | « Terminé · 1 245 analysés, 3 illisibles · 6 min 49 s »; unreadable counts the empty and the failed files (neither can answer a question yet); nothing new says so; a stopped pass says that what was read is kept. The duration is measured by the client from the press, not taken from `timings` |
| The 2 px line | Built behind the `PROGRESS_HAIRLINE` constant, **on** by default (the screenshot has it). Turning it off is a one-word edit, so the owner decides by looking at the real window |
| Tables | The gauge is shown for the tabular pass as for the documents. A plain spinner below a threshold stays in reserve, not built: decide at the human test whether a pass of a second or two is noisy |
| Old bar | `AnalysisProgress` and its CSS stay for the two old cards until step 10 |
| Human test played (owner, 20 October 2026) | Played on her real state (Documents = the test folder, Tables pointed at the 510-workbook test folder, server on the tower): the gauge, the stages, the Stop of each family, the two passes at once with the window staying responsive, the thin line, the end-of-pass lines: **all as expected, « very conclusive »**. The whole documents pass lasted 9 min 45 s with 1 248 documents; the reading stage took about 5 min, then the names, then the meaning search. Her index and settings were restored from the backup |
| Findings of that test | (1) At the narrowest width the times of the line are cut by the ellipsis, as designed, so the remaining time was not readable until the window was widened. (2) Two notes of the last index (« L'index doit être refait », « Recherche par sens : 0 % ») stayed drawn during the pass and contradicted the gauge: **now hidden while the family runs**. (3) The left column could not be widened beyond 35 % |
| Left column up to 50 % in two columns | Asked by the owner: the explorer's column may take **half the window when the centre is closed**; with the centre open it stays at 35 % (three zones share the window). `AppLayout.tsx`: the Resources panel's `maxSize` follows `centreOpen`. The stored triples are unchanged (the closed-centre triple simply may hold a larger first share); a saved width above 35 % never applies to the open centre, whose own triple is used |
