## Taken after the owner's test of step 6 (20 October 2026, evening), to confirm

The owner played the human test of step 6 on her real state. The overview, « Voir le rapport », « Voir », the note saved from the Notes section (the pass starts by itself and nothing opens by force) and the restore all behaved as expected. Her remarks, all treated in the same session at her request:

| Subject | Taken |
| --- | --- |
| The reason of an unreadable file | « Raison connue après une analyse. » was too vague. The index already knows: a document is **unreadable only when it was read and held no text** (a row with no passage), and the inventory records whether text recognition ran on it (`FileRecord.indexMetadata.ocrEngine`). Measured on a copy of her index: her three unreadable PDFs were **all run through Tesseract and returned nothing**. The reasons the page can now show, in order of precedence: (1) **an error of the last pass** through the catalogue: `extraction_failed` (« Ce fichier n'a pas pu être lu »: damaged, protected or open elsewhere), `embedding_timeout`, `server_error`, `provider_error` (and its `timeout` reason): the AI side failed, the next pass retries; (2) **too little legible text** (`lowConfidence`): « Trop peu lisible pour être indexé. »; (3) **recognised**: « La reconnaissance de texte n'a trouvé aucun texte lisible. » (text recognition ran, found nothing: a blank or unreadable scan); (4) **empty**: « Aucun texte trouvé dans ce fichier. » (read, no text, no recognition: an empty file); (5) **no table** (a workbook): « Aucun tableau exploitable. ». These need no pass in this session. `unknown` is gone |
| The lists never fold or unfold on their own | Her remark: the two lists seemed to open by themselves when she removed the « Illisible » pill. The code has exactly two writers of a list's fold: the arrow button of each family and the overview's link; removing a pill, changing a filter or a pass finishing write none (a source guard in `explorerView.test.ts` pins that). What she most likely saw: **a list's height follows its rows**, so when the filter is removed an almost empty list (« Aucun fichier ne correspond. ») grows to its row budget, which looks like an opening. **To confirm with her.** Changed anyway: the link no longer unfolds both lists, only the folded ones that hold some of the files it counts (`familiesToUnfold`, pure and tested), so with her three PDFs only Documents opens |
| The folder in the header | The mock-up (state 3) showed the short folder name in grey after the family's name (« …\Docs »); the first build moved the path to the tooltip only. Back, with `abbreviateFolderPath`: the name stays whole, the folder's short name is what gives way (ellipsis), the whole path remains the tooltip. Class `explorer-family__location` (not `__folder`, which a test reading `explorer-family__fold` would take for the fold button) |
| The whole path on the overview | Each family's **whole folder path** is shown in its card, before and after the first analysis, as important as the counts and the date; it wraps (`overflow-wrap: anywhere`) so it never widens the page |
| The search that found nothing for « Notes/2026 » (finder, Rust) | A saved note is `Notes/2026-10-20_18-51-28.md`. Typing `Notes/2026` found nothing while the whole name found it. Cause: the words of a name leave the **date prefix out** (`2026-10-20` is a date, not words), so `2026` was neither a word of the name nor, being beside `notes`, a typed date. Fixed in `NameProfile::matches_as`: **the pieces of the date a name starts with, and of the year or month a folder is, count as words** (`2026`, `10`, `20`), so `notes 2026`, `Notes/2026-10`, `notes 2026 10 20` and the start `Notes/20` find the note; `notes 2025`, `Notes/2026-11` and `notes 2026 10 21` do not. `all_words` (used by the ranking) is unchanged. A date typed in front of the name still has to be the file's own date, as before. Test `the_pieces_of_the_date_a_name_starts_with_are_words_beside_other_words`; the finder's integration tests (`file_finder`, `finder_names`, `finder_rank_profile`, `finder_long`) pass |
| 510 tableurs retirés | Correct and expected: her Tables folder is `C:\Users\elise\AssistantCabinetAI\Data` (15 workbooks); the 510 are the workbooks of the test folder analysed earlier, which are not in this folder, so a pass forgets them (and her restore brings them back). Her left box said **18 to analyse** at the start: 15 of her folder plus 3 files of the Docs folder listed under Tables (« depuis Docs »); after the pass 15 analysed and 3 to analyse |

## Taken after the owner's second test of step 6 (20 October 2026, night), to confirm

Her re-test: header folder names, full paths and reasons validated (« the folder name stays visible at the narrowest width, we keep it »); the finder fix works (`Notes/2026-10-20` finds the saved note once analysed). New remarks, investigated in the code:

| Subject | Found / taken |
| --- | --- |
| A click on a file's name no longer ticked it | A regression of the new box: only the checkbox ticked (the old lists ticked on the whole row). **Fixed:** a click anywhere on a tickable row ticks it (`rowClickTicks`, pure and tested; the checkbox and the Ouvrir / Voir buttons keep their own behaviour; `cursor: pointer` on tickable rows only). Foreign, unanalysed and unreadable rows, and every row while a question is being answered, are not ticked by a click. The checkbox stays the keyboard's way |
| Why « Notes » targeted other files | Not case or plural: her test folder already holds a real folder **`administratif/notes/` with 85 « note-interne » files**, and the finder reads a folder's words as words of the file (`folder_words`). `Notes/2026-10-20` therefore matched every file of that folder whose date the words fitted. The reason label said « dans le nom » for a hit that was really the folder (a mislabel, not fixed yet) |
| Why « -20 » did not narrow | The last typed word is read as the **start** of a word while typing (`20` starts `2026`), and since the previous fix the pieces of a name's date are words. **Fixed in `NameProfile::matches_as`:** a **whole date typed anywhere in the query** (`notes/2026-10-20`, `2026-10-20 notes`) is read as a date, the file's own date must equal it (it is no longer four words), and a typed **month** (`2026-10`) must be the month of the file's date or of its folder. `Notes/2026-10-20` now finds only the note of that day; `notes 2026-10` no longer finds a February 10. Partial dates still read as words while being typed. Tests in `file_profile/name.rs`; the finder's integration tests pass |
| The best match is last in the list | The box sorted each family **by name**, so the finder's ranking was not shown: the saved note was the last of six. **Chosen by the owner and built:** while a text search is active the lists follow the finder's ranking (`sortEntries(..., "relevance", rank)`; the position each finder gave its own folder's files; an unranked file goes last, by name). Ties keep the finder's order (path); a newest-first tie-break in the finder is not built |
| Lists opening when a filter pill is removed | Investigated, **not a bug in the filter code**: her lists were folded, the link « Afficher les N… » **unfolded Documents for good** (the fold setting was written, as documented), then removing the pill let that already-open list grow from 3 rows to its full budget. No code path folds or unfolds a list on a pill, a filter or a pass; the guard test stays. The behaviour she expected (the list goes back to folded when the filter is removed) is a different rule. **Chosen by the owner and built:** the link opens a folded list that holds the files, and **removing the filter it set closes what it opened, and only that** (`foldsToRestore`, `linkOpened` in `App`); a fold she makes herself with the arrow drops that memory, so the application never closes a list she opened or closed. The other options (keep the permanent unfold; never unfold and show « N fichiers correspondent ») were declined |
| Notes must be easy to find (owner, « TRÈS IMPORTANT ») | The user writes a note precisely to find it again quickly; a note buried among 1 200 files is a product defect. Four proposals were made. **Chosen and built:** (1) a one-click **« Mes notes (N) »** chip under the segmented control, shown while the Notes folder (setting `speech.notesFolderName`, inside the Documents folder) holds a file; it toggles that folder as an origin-folder choice (so it is a pill, combines with the other filters, includes sub-folders, and does not match `administratif/notes`); with that choice alone and no text search the Documents list is **newest first**; (2) relevance order (previous row). **Not chosen, left open:** a useful default note name (date + first words, e.g. `2026-10-20_rdv-dr-martin.md`), and a « Mes notes » list with « Utiliser cette note » in the Notes section. They belong to a Notes-section step, to schedule with the owner; step 7's built-in views may carry a Notes view too |
| The bottom bar stays at two lines with nothing used (owner, third look) | With no file used the bar had three lines (« Utilisés : aucun », the warning sentence, the unread-format note), which made the box grow a scroll bar. The warning is now the short **« Aucun fichier pour l'IA »** (key `explorer.noteNone`), on the **same line** as « Utilisés : aucun », so the bar is two lines at most with an unread-format note and one without. The whole-family warnings (« Tous les documents analysés sont utilisés. ») keep their own line |

## Taken while building the explorer's knowledge-base filters, views and automatic selection (21 October 2026), confirmed by the owner's test

Handoff `EXPLORER-07-kb-filters.md`. The specification of the three items is the owner's; the choices below are the ones the handoff left open or that the real data forced. Nothing here changes how entities are extracted.

| Subject | Taken |
| --- | --- |
| Where the counts come from | **Not from the engine facets.** A result's facets cannot leave the kind's own choices out (the step 3 rule) and list ten entities at most, with no type. One new **read-only command, `file_knowledge_facts(domain)`** (`finder/facts.rs`), returns once per folder the business type, the years, and the people and organisations with their roles of every analysed file, plus the names of those entities; the explorer works every count and every filter out itself (`explorerFacts.ts`, `explorerFilters.ts`). A filter change costs **no call**. The finder, the engine and `find_files` are unchanged (only `name_date` became `pub(super)`) |
| Cost, measured | On a copy of her analysed index (1,246 documents, 411 entities listed): **about 55 ms warm, 218 ms cold, in a debug build**, 325 KB of JSON; the workbooks' folder 1 to 6 ms. Read only when the panel is open, when the « Vues » side is shown or when a knowledge-base choice is in force; again after a pass and each time the panel opens; never at launch (`useKnowledgeFacts`). A failed read leaves the folder without facts and nothing else breaks |
| What counts as « linked » | The roles of the file's profile **and** the knowledge base's mentions (`mention` when it gave no role, else `client`, `provider`, `supplier`): the engine reads the profile alone for a role, here a role is any role a link has, which is what the second step offers. A name with no labelled role (only `mention`) has no second step. Candidates count as names; merged entities answer as their survivor; deleted ones link nothing; types other than person and organisation are not listed. Years: the document's date, the date its name starts with, and every year a workbook's date columns span |
| Several names in one field | **« This or that »**, like every other kind (step 3 rule); the kinds combine as « this and that ». The engine's own entity filter is an intersection; the explorer is not the engine's request |
| Roles | One choice per entity: « Tous les rôles » or one role (`12` or `12:author`); choosing a role replaces the earlier choice of that entity. Labels: `explorer.role.<code>` (subject, issuer, « Cité » for the profile's `mentioned`), else `knowledge.roles.<code>`; a role with no label and `mention` are not offered |
| What a view stores | **Filters only**: the family and the panel's choices (formats, states, origin folders, people, organisations, types, years) plus the two switches the built-in views need, `recent` (newest first, which wins over the finder's ranking since the user chose it and a pill says so) and `inConversation` (only the files the conversation uses now). Never a file, a selection, the typed text or a fold. In `settings.json`, key `explorerViews` (`SavedView`), read leniently entry by entry (unusable entries dropped, ids unique, at most 50, names cut at 60, unknown states dropped), sanitised again on save; Rust round-trip, default and corruption tests |
| Names are never written into the settings file | A person or an organisation is kept **by id**. A view that names one the files no longer link to says « Un nom de ce filtre ne figure plus dans vos fichiers. » and the pill reads « Nom introuvable »; a folder that no longer exists reads « (introuvable) ». The entity ids can change when the knowledge base is reset: such a view then says so |
| Opening a view | Replaces the filters through `onViewChange` and returns to « Fichiers »; it **keeps the typed text** (decided, documented: the text stays visible in its field, and a view that must keep an extension stores it as a format choice), the selection and every fold. The step 6 rule stays: no view, filter, pill or pass folds or unfolds a list (the guard test is unchanged) |
| Built-in views | **Récents** (newest first), **À analyser** (state), **Dans la conversation** (the files the conversation uses now). Their names are catalogue keys; they are not stored |
| « Select. auto » | Exactly the owner's specification, as the pure machine `lib/autoSelect.ts`. Decisions where it was silent: a switch turned on again takes a **new** snapshot; the manual change keeps the snapshot (« drops nothing else »); restoring consumes it; a result with no filter at all is every analysed file (« tous »), the bar's existing whole-family warning then says it; the results that cannot be ticked are those not analysed **or** in the other family's folder; a result that arrives during a question is followed when it ends, if the switch is still on. Nothing is stored, so it is off at every launch, and it resets if the box is unmounted |
| What the real index showed | On the copy of her index the most frequent person is the practice doctor (700 of 1,246 files, all only as « mention »), and one « person » is a town name (« Annemasse Annemasse », 80 files): both come from the extraction, out of scope here, and the frequency order puts them first as specified. They are the reason a per-person automatic view and a selectivity cut in this list were left out; to decide with the owner |

## Settled by the owner at the design spike of the explorer's step 8: root and family (21 October 2026)

Handoff `EXPLORER-08-in-place.md`, item 1. Two options were written; **the owner chose option C on 21 October 2026**, before any code. Option A stays in the table as the rejected alternative.

| Subject | Proposed |
| --- | --- |
| Two words, never mixed again | **Root** = the physical folder a file sits in (`documents` or `data`, the two settings). **Family** = how the file is read (the document pipeline or the tabular pipeline), decided by its extension (`FileKind`). `Domain` in the code (`file_profile::build::Domain`, the `domain` columns) **already means the family** in every stored row: a document of the Data folder is never stored today, it is only shown as failed by the Data inventory. So `domain` stays as it is |
| What the stored keys are today | `documents.relative_path`, `tabular_inventories.relative_path`, `chunks.relative_path`, `document_cards`, `doc_structure`, `doc_sections`, `chunk_index_text`, `chunk_sections`, the OCR resume cache, `file_profiles` and `kb_sources` (unique on `(domain, relative_path)`) all identify a file by its path **inside its native root**. The chunk id is `<relative_path>#p<page>#s<section>`. Two files with the same path in the two roots, and the same family, would collide in all of them |
| Option A (the handoff's recommendation, rejected): a `root` column | Add `root` to every table above and to their keys, and change the chunk id. Measured on the code: `relative_path` appears 202 times in `index_store.rs`, 67 in `file_profile/store.rs`, 59 in `knowledge/store.rs`, 242 times in the integration tests, plus the retrieval, finder, tabular and knowledge modules; SQLite cannot alter a `UNIQUE` or a primary key, so every table is rebuilt. An existing index is **not valid** afterwards: the next Analyse reads her 1,252 documents again (about 10 minutes plus the embeddings on the tower, and her saved views that name a person or an organisation say « Un nom de ce filtre ne figure plus dans vos fichiers. » until the names exist again). Typed (`root` in the key), so the compiler finds every site |
| **Option C (chosen): the root lives in the key of a foreign file, no schema change** | A file keeps the key it has today **when it sits in the root of its own family** (a document in the Documents folder, a workbook in the Data folder): every existing row stays valid, byte for byte. A **foreign** file, that is a workbook found in the Documents folder or a document found in the Data folder, is stored with the key **`/<root>/<path inside that root>`** (`/documents/Factures/2025.xlsx`, `/data/Courriers/a.pdf`) under the family that reads it. One small module (`file_key.rs`: `FileKey`, `from_root`, `parse`, `native_root_of(family)`) is the only place that builds or reads that form. Nothing is added to any table |
| Why a leading slash | (1) A native key is a relative path made of plain names, so it **never starts with `/`**: no file can be mistaken for a foreign one, whatever its name (a colon, as in `data:x`, is legal on macOS). (2) Every resolver that touches the disk (`reveal::resolve_plain_file`, so the viewer, the file manager, the allow-list) rebuilds a path from plain names and **refuses a root component**: a foreign key given to code that does not know about roots **fails closed** (« unreadable »), it can never open another file. Step 9 makes the resolvers root-aware. (3) `rsplit('/')` still gives the file name and the parent still gives a folder (`/data/Courriers`), so the code that cuts a key keeps working |
| What C means for the passes | The two walks (`discovery::discover_all`) already return the absolute path of each file. The document pass takes its files from **both** roots and gives each a `relative_path` equal to its key; the tabular pass does the same. Extraction, hashing, OCR, chunking and the tabular reader read `absolute_path` and need no change. `retain_documents` and the forgetting of vanished workbooks receive the union of both roots' keys. Each file is resolved against **its own root**, canonicalised, no link followed, exactly as today; the hash caches stay one per root |
| What C means for the interface | The explorer lists files per root with plain paths, as today (`explorerKey(root, path)`). The index speaks keys. One pure function pair, `indexKey(root, family, path)` and `parseIndexKey(family, key)` in `lib/`, and the same in `file_key.rs`, translate at the three borders: the inventories (which join the index by key), the facts (`file_knowledge_facts` returns keys; `joinFacts` places each by its root) and the scopes (an entry of `mode` or `dataMode` carries the key; its root is read from it). The chat contract is unchanged |
| The index is never rebuilt | Under C there is nothing to detect or to rebuild: the index written before step 8 is valid as it is, and a pass that meets no foreign file writes exactly what it wrote before (a test compares the rows). The handoff's « old index detected at launch, rebuilt by the next Analyse » therefore **falls away**, and with it the effect on saved views |
| Cost, estimated | A: the whole 15 h of the handoff and more (schema, rebuild, 240 test sites, client). C: schema work disappears; the 15 h shrink by about 5 h, and the risk goes with it (the tabular snapshot stays byte-identical by construction, not by proof alone). Both are estimates, unmeasured |
| What C gives up | A string convention instead of a typed column: a new module that joins a key to a root by hand would meet a key starting with `/` and refuse it (fail closed), but only a test finds it. Mitigation: `FileKey` is the only constructor, a guard test lists the modules allowed to read `relative_path` as a path, and the refusal is pinned by a test of `resolve_plain_file` on a key |
| Taken whatever the option | (a) The **document pass renames a document wherever it sits** (both roots), same log, same guarantees; the **tabular pass renames nothing** (today it renames the documents of the Data folder; that moves to the document pass). (b) Two roots that are equal or nested are refused on validation and on load (`settings.rs`, a new machine code and a sentence in both catalogues). (c) The summaries of both passes count a file as analysed when it is indexed **and** current. (d) The finder serves a foreign file in the search of the folder it physically sits in, with the profile of its own family read under its key |

## Taken while building the explorer's analysis in place (21 October 2026), to confirm

Handoff `EXPLORER-08-in-place.md`, on the design chosen above (option C). Built; to confirm with the owner when she has played the human test. Rust and TypeScript; no schema change.

| Subject | Taken |
| --- | --- |
| Where the rules live | Rust: `file_key.rs` (`Root`, `index_key`, `parse_key`, `plain_path`, `family_of_extension`, `FamilyRoots::resolve`: the only code that builds or reads a foreign key), `family_view.rs` (each family across both folders), `inventory.rs` (`discover_in`, `tabular_state`), `discovery.rs` (`discover_foreign`, `discover_family`). TypeScript: `lib/indexKey.ts` (the twin, pure and tested), `explorerSelection.ts` (scopes by family). `FileRecord` and `ScopeEntry` gained **no** field: the key of the file says its folder (`parseIndexKey`), which replaces the `root` field the handoff wrote |
| No schema change, no rebuild | The `CREATE TABLE` blocks are untouched. An index written before this step is valid as it is (measured on a copy of her real index, below). A pass that meets no file of the other family writes the same rows as before (tests compare the tabular tables, the documents, the passages and the profiles). The handoff's « old index detected at launch and rebuilt by the next Analyse » therefore has nothing to detect, and her saved views keep working (they hold folder keys `<root>:<folder>` and entity ids, none of which moved) |
| The two passes | `indexing::run_cancellable` and `data_folder::analyse_cancellable` take the other folder as `Option<&Path>` (the commands read it from the settings); the wrappers `run`, `run_with_knowledge`, `analyse`, `analyse_with_knowledge` keep their signatures and read their own folder alone. The file list of a pass is its own folder's files of its family plus `discover_foreign` of the other folder, sorted by key; extraction, hashing, OCR, chunking and the tabular reader read the real absolute path, unchanged. `retain_documents` and `retain_tabular_inventories` receive the union, so a foreign file that leaves its folder is forgotten by its own pass (and only when the pass was not stopped). The hash caches are one per folder. The other folder is resolved only if the settings name it: a key naming a folder that is not chosen resolves to nothing. Symbolic links and `..` are refused exactly as before |
| The profile and the names | A foreign file's profile is stored under its family and its key. Its name is made of its own path, never of the folder it was found in: `NameProfile::build` drops the leading `/<folder>/` of a key, so `data` and `documents` never become words of a name, in the finder's on-the-fly profile of an unanalysed file either (test in `file_profile/name.rs`) |
| Inventories of a folder (the explorer) | `WorkFolderInventory::discover_in(root, ...)` joins each file to what **its own family** made of it, by its key: a document to the document index, a workbook to the tabular inventories. A workbook of the Documents folder is green once the tabular pass read it, a document of the Data folder once the document pass did. A file no pipeline reads stays « not assessed » in the Documents folder and **unreadable in the Data folder**, as before (the old rule « a document in Data is forced to failed » is gone; two tests that described it were rewritten) |
| Families across both folders (the conversation) | `family_view::family_views` composes the two folders' inventories into the documents family (the Documents folder's files unchanged, plus the Data folder's documents under their keys) and the tables family (the Data folder's files, plus the Documents folder's workbooks under their keys, with their cached inventories). Every place of `commands.rs` that built an inventory for a question, a selection, a suggestion or the picker uses it through `family_views_of`; the explorer's own listing commands stay per folder. « Tous » means every analysed file of the family, wherever it sits. Cost: two walks (one per folder) whichever family a question needs, with the application's hash caches. **Limit:** a file of the other folder reaches the model, the sources list and the folder-question answers under its key (`/data/...`); opening it from a source is step 9 and the viewer refuses a key (`ViewerFileUnreadable`) instead of opening another file |
| The finder is by family | `find_files(domain)` takes the family; its file list is `discover_family` (the family's own folder with plain paths, the other folder's files of the family with keys), so a workbook of the Documents folder is searched with the tables, with its profile read under its key. The client asks once per family and places each hit by the folder its key names (`foundKeys`, `rankByKey`, `reasonsByKey` use `explorerKeyOfIndexKey`). A file of the other family is no longer in a folder's search by name: it is in its family's. The text typed is sent to both families as before. `file_knowledge_facts(domain)` was already per family: it returns keys and the client joins them by the same function (`joinFacts`) |
| Scopes | One per family, as before, each entry under the file's key: `mode` holds the documents wherever they sit, `dataMode` the workbooks wherever they sit; the chat contract is unchanged. `ScopeEntry::find_in` resolves an entry by its key, then, when the path is gone, by the single file that still has the content it pinned: **a renamed document or workbook keeps its place**. Two copies of the pinned content are not guessed between (the entry is missing); two entries resolving to one file count once; a sheet restriction follows the resolved path. A path with the wrong content is `changed`, as before |
| Names of files | `filename_sanitizer::sanitize_documents` renames the documents of the Documents folder **and** of the Data folder (reported under their key, logged with it, recorded in the profile's original names under the documents family); the tabular pass renames nothing (it used to rename the documents of the Data folder). Workbooks are never renamed: a test pins `.xlsx`, `.csv`, `.ods`, `.xls`, `.xlsm`, with spaces and accents, in both folders. The misleading comment of `index_data_folder` is gone |
| Two folders that overlap | New machine code `folders_overlap` (both catalogues). `settings::save` refuses two chosen folders that are equal or nested; `load_app_snapshot` sets the Data folder aside **in memory** (the file is not rewritten) and reports the warning, like a folder that no longer passes the rules. Compared by components with the platform's case rule (`work_folder::folders_overlap`); a shared start of name is not a shared folder **Superseded by the owner's decision of 21 October 2026 (evening), see « Settled by the owner after the test of step 8 »: step 8B lifts it.** |
| Hidden and system files | The walk lists every regular file; the family is decided by the extension alone. So an Office lock file (`~$Suivi.xlsx`) is read by the tabular pass and reported as a workbook with no table; `.DS_Store` (no extension), `Thumbs.db` and `desktop.ini` belong to no family and are neither read nor reported as an unread format. No rename touches a hidden file or a lock file (already the rule). Pinned by `hidden_and_system_files_follow_the_extension_rule_and_are_never_renamed`. Not changed: showing `~$` lock files as unreadable workbooks is the existing behaviour, to decide later if it annoys |
| « Analysed » in the summaries | `InventorySummary.indexed_files` (the Documents card, and the model's folder context) now counts the files whose status is `Indexed`, that is **read and current**; before, it counted every file with passages, stale ones included, while the Data report counted current ones only. The counts of a **pass** (`IndexSummary`) stay what they were: files read and usable **in that pass** (the document pass skips unchanged files, the tabular pass rebuilds every workbook every time, by design) |
| Client rules | The state of a file is its inventory's verdict (the forced « to analyse » of a foreign file is gone). `isTickable` is « analysed », wherever it sits; the new `isOpenable` keeps the old gate (analysed and in the folder of its family) for Ouvrir, and Voir stays gated on the folder of its family: both wait for step 9. The family's **Analyser (n)** counts its foreign files; the tooltip and the catalogue keys `foreignState`, `foreignExcludedOne`, `foreignExcludedMany` are removed, `foreignHint` now says the file is analysed where it is. The « À vérifier » page lists a foreign file that is unreadable or that its pass failed to read |
| Measured on a copy of her real index (21 October 2026) | Settings read first: Documents `C:\Users\elise\AssistantCabinetAI-test\Docs` (1,253 files, 1,249 documents, 3 workbooks `administratif/commandes/2026-02-06_commandes.csv`, `2026-04-21_commandes.xlsx`, `2026-08-09_commandes.xlsx`, one `.ppt`), Data `C:\Users\elise\AssistantCabinetAI\Data` (17 files: 15 workbooks and two documents, `Test/publipostage/modele_lettre.docx` and `.txt`). Before any pass: Documents 1,246 analysed, 3 unreadable, 2 to analyse (the letters of Data); Tables 15 analysed, 3 to analyse (the workbooks of Docs). Tabular pass: 18 read, 18 green, nothing forgotten. Document pass: 1,251 scanned, 2 written, 1,249 unchanged, nothing failed, nothing forgotten (about 75 s in a debug build, the copy and her tower's embeddings included). After: Documents 1,248 analysed, Tables 18 analysed. The facts of the foreign files: the two letters are of type `letter` (the `.docx` dated 2013, with one person or organisation; the `.txt` with two), the three workbooks of type `stock` (years 2025-2026). Search « commandes »: 0 documents, 3 tables (the three workbooks of Docs); « modele lettre »: 2 documents, 0 tables; « publipostage »: 2 documents (the letters) and 4 tables (the four workbooks of Data with that name) |

## Owner's test of the explorer's analysis in place (21 October 2026), findings and open questions

The owner played the human test of step 8 on her real state (Documents `AssistantCabinetAI-test\Docs`, Tables `AssistantCabinetAI\Data`, AI server on the tower). Recorded as played; **the code was left as it was** until the owner's answer (then only F-1 was fixed).

| Part | Result |
| --- | --- |
| 0, 1 (backup, start numbers) | As predicted: 1,270 files before; Documents 1,246 analysed, 2 to analyse, 3 unreadable; Tables 15, 3, 0 |
| 2 (tabular pass over the three workbooks of Docs) | The pass ended (« Terminé · 18 analysés · 36 s ») but the header and the overview kept **15 analysed** until the window lost and regained the focus; then 18, 0, 0 as predicted. **Defect, see F-1** |
| 3 (document pass over the two letters of Data) | Same behaviour: « Terminé · 2 analysés · 39 s », counts stale until the focus came back, then Documents 1,248 analysed, 0 to analyse, 3 unreadable as predicted. **F-1** again |
| 4 (search) | As predicted (`commandes`: 3 tables « depuis Docs »; `modele lettre`: 2 documents « depuis Data ») |
| 5 (table question on a workbook of Docs) | « Somme de Montant : 2 449,82. Calculé sur 12 lignes », computed without the AI; the source reads `/documents/administratif/commandes/2026-02-06_commandes.csv` (the known limit: the key is shown, step 9) |
| 6 (document question on a letter of Data) | The passage and the source come back, `/data/Test/publipostage/modele_lettre.txt, page 1` (the key shown, same limit); generated by ministral-3:3b in 3 s |
| 7 (nothing moved) | `identiques`: no file of either folder was added, renamed or modified |
| 8 (restore) | Done |
| 9 (nested folders refused) | The refusal and its sentence appear as designed. **The owner questions the rule itself, see Q-1** |

**F-1: the counts are stale after a pass until the window gets the focus back (cause found, not fixed).** Cause, in `FileExplorer.tsx`: each folder's inventory is read again only when **its own** pass finishes (`useFolderInventory(..., indexing.finishedPasses)` for the Documents folder, `dataIndexing.finishedPasses` for the Data folder). Since step 8 the state of a file of the other family lives in the **other folder's** inventory: the three workbooks of Docs are in the Documents inventory, the two letters of Data in the Data inventory. A tabular pass therefore re-reads the Data inventory only and leaves the Documents inventory, which holds the three workbooks, as it was; the focus event re-reads both, which is why the counts then came right. The same gap exists for « Effacer l'analyse » of one family (`folders.clears`) and in the two old cards of the Settings. The fix is to re-read both inventories after any pass or clear (the finder and the facts already depend on both counters). **Fixed on the owner's go (21 October 2026):** `lib/inventoryRefresh.ts` (pure, tested) gives both inventories the sum of the two families' finished passes and of the clears, so either pass or clear re-reads both. The two old cards of the Settings keep the gap until step 10 removes them. To check by the owner: after each Analyser the counters and the overview are right without leaving the window.

**Q-1: should two equal or nested folders stay refused?** Asked by the owner after part 9, with the argument that a user may keep patient or client folders mixing texts and spreadsheets and should not have to reorganise them, that the application could already process them, and that before step 8 the same folder could be chosen twice. The analysis is in the answer given to her; the owner **decided the same evening to allow every combination**, with a guarantee against duplicates: see the next section. Until step 8B is built, the rule of « Taken while building the explorer's analysis in place » (refused on save, set aside on load) stands.

## Settled by the owner after the test of step 8: any two folders, never a duplicate (21 October 2026, evening)

Decided in conversation after the human test of step 8 (previous section). What was asked and answered, in order:

1. **Refresh.** The owner asked that an analysis update the counters and the overview **without the window losing and regaining the focus**. Done the same day (`lib/inventoryRefresh.ts`, finding F-1).
2. **Should two equal or nested folders stay refused?** The owner's argument: a user may already keep patient or client folders mixing texts and spreadsheets and should not have to reorganise them; the application can now process a file wherever it sits; before step 8 the same folder could be chosen twice; the separation of the two types teaches how the application works but must not be imposed. Her question: does it really cause a problem, and what would happen if the restriction were lifted?
3. **Analysis given.** Without a precaution, a file reachable from both folders would be read twice, under two keys: vectors computed twice, duplicate hits in the search, duplicate sources in an answer, « tous » counting twice; no data loss and no security issue. With the precaution « **a file found by walking the other folder is never read as foreign when it already lies under the folder of its own family** », the three cases are handled by one rule: same folder (each family reads its own type there, nothing is foreign, no « depuis … » tag), tables inside documents (the documents of the whole tree are the document pass's, the workbooks of the sub-folder are the tabular pass's, the workbooks elsewhere in the tree are read in place), documents inside tables (the same the other way round).
4. **A half restriction was considered and rejected** (same folder allowed, nested refused): it saves about half an hour of tests and adds a validation rule, the duplicate check is needed for the same folder anyway and covers the nested cases at no extra cost, and « the same folder yes, a folder inside the other no » is hard to explain while the nested layout (a `Tableaux` sub-folder of a client folder) is probably the most natural one.
5. **Decision (owner, 21 October 2026): allow every combination** (separate, identical, tables inside documents, documents inside tables) **and make a duplicate impossible by construction, with tests in the code and a human test**. Done on **a separate branch**, `feat/explorer-nested-folders` (handoff `docs/SESSION-EXPLORER/EXPLORER-08B-same-or-nested-folders.md`), **before** step 9. The separate `Docs` and `Data` folders stay the **suggested default** for a new user.

**What this supersedes.** The rule « two folders that are equal or nested are refused when chosen and set aside on load » (`folders_overlap`, error code `folders_overlap`, its two catalogue sentences, the `folderError` banner of the box, the warning at load) taken while building step 8 at the request of the handoff of that step. Step 8 as committed had it; step 8B removed it (see "Taken while building the explorer's any-two-folders step", below).

**The rule step 8B must build, in one sentence.** One file has **one key**, decided in one place from its absolute path and the two folders: under the folder of its own family (a document under the Documents folder, a workbook under the Data folder) its path inside that folder; otherwise, under the other folder, `/<root>/<path>`; under neither, it is not read. Where the two folders overlap, a file lying under both takes the key of its own family's folder. Every walk, every join and every search goes through that one decision, and a file is never listed twice under two keys.

**Questions of the owner kept for the record.** « Does each indexing process take care of the files that concern it, wherever they are? » Yes, since step 8: the document pass reads the documents and the tabular pass the workbooks, in both folders. The only thing 8B adds is that a file reachable from both folders is read once.

## Taken while building the explorer's any-two-folders step (21 October 2026), confirmed by the owner's test

Handoff `EXPLORER-08B-same-or-nested-folders.md`, on the owner's decision above. Built, then confirmed by the owner's human test (next section). Rust and TypeScript; no schema change.

| Subject | Taken |
| --- | --- |
| The rule | `file_key::key_under(family, own, other, absolute)` (and `key_for` with the folders given as Documents and Data, `FamilyRoots::key_of`): under the family's own folder, the path inside it; else under the other folder, `/<root>/<path>`; else `None`. A file under both takes the first. Components are compared with `work_folder::components_for_comparison` (case folded on Windows), never by prefix; `..` and any non-name component give `None`. |
| Who calls it | `discovery::discover_family` (now the only walk of both folders: the document pass, the tabular pass and the finder call it; `discover_foreign` is gone), `WorkFolderInventory::discover_in` (new parameter: the other folder), `family_view::family_views`, `filename_sanitizer::sanitize_documents`. `discover_alongside` on `WorkFolderInventory` and `DataFolder` give the two explorer commands the other folder; `discover`, `discover_with_cache` keep their signatures for the tests that never had a second folder. |
| Belt and braces | A list of files is de-duplicated on `file_key::physical_identity` (components, platform case rule) before any key is written, in `discover_family` and in `family_views`; the clean-name report no longer lists a file twice either. A mutation that removes the de-duplication makes three of the new tests fail (checked). |
| The refusal is gone | `folders_overlap`, its check in `settings::save` and `load_app_snapshot`, `AppError::FoldersOverlap`, the two catalogue sentences, the `folderError` prop and its banner, and the test that pinned it. `work_folder::components_for_comparison` became crate-visible for the rule. |
| What the document pass sends to the gateway | One text per passage **and one description per document** (`Title: ...`): the number of vectors asked is the passages plus the documents, so the test checks that every text is sent once and that the total is exactly that, in each layout. |
| Changing the folders | Separate to same: the next document pass forgets the `/data/...` documents (they are not in its list) and the next tabular pass forgets the old Data workbooks and the `/documents/...` keys; the workbooks of the Documents folder are read natively. Tested: no key starting with a slash is left in `documents`, `tabular_inventories`, `file_profiles` or `kb_sources`. |
| The explorer | The two inventories give a shared file the same key and state; `mergeInventories` keeps one entry, the native copy (or the Documents one), and counts a file no pipeline reads once. The tag « depuis ... » shows only on a really foreign file, so never in the same-folder layout. Saved views and the origin-folder filter are unchanged (`<root>:<folder>`). |

## Taken while building the actions on any file (explorer step 9, 21 October 2026)

Step 9 (`docs/SESSION-EXPLORER/EXPLORER-09-actions.md`) was far smaller than estimated: step 8 had already routed the open and reveal calls by the entry's physical root and made the ticking and the scope work by family. What was left was two gates and the way a key is shown.

| Subject | Decision |
| --- | --- |
| Ouvrir | `isOpenable(entry)` is `entry.state === "analysed"`, wherever the file sits. The explorer opens it by its root and plain path (`entry.root`, `entry.path`), which `read_work_file_for_viewer` and `read_data_file_for_viewer` already accept for any extension. **No Rust signature changed**, so no Rust test was added |
| Voir | Offered for **every** file of the explorer and of the « A verifier » page (`checkActions().reveal` is `true`), whatever its state and its folder; the reveal commands take a path inside their root and are extension-agnostic. The old `entry.native` gates are gone |
| Tab identity | Unchanged: `tabId(folder, path)` with the **root** as folder. In every layout of step 8B a physical file has exactly one entry (`mergeInventories`), so it has one root and can never be two tabs; the same path in the two roots stays two tabs. Tested in `viewerTabs.test.ts` and `explorerModel.test.ts` (tables inside documents and documents inside tables, the N-4 item) |
| Viewer guard | Unchanged in code. It compares a tab with the inventory of **its own folder**, and that inventory joins a file to what its own family made of it (step 8), so a foreign file is closed only when it is really missing, no longer analysed or modified. Tested with a workbook in the Documents root and a letter in the Data root |
| Keys the user reads | The source lines under an answer used to show the key (`/documents/administratif/commandes/2026-02-06_commandes.csv`, the known limit recorded in step 8). They now show the path inside the folder (`displayPathOfKey`, `lib/indexKey.ts`). Nothing in the conversation opens a file from a source (they are text lines), so no « open a source » path was added; if one is added, it goes through `parseIndexKey` first. The knowledge-base sheets (`EntitySheetParts.tsx`, `ReviewTab.tsx`) still show a key as it is: out of the scope of this step, noted for the knowledge work |
| Her state at the time of the step | Her settings point at `C:/Users/elise/AssistantCabinetAI-test/Docs` and `C:/Users/elise/AssistantCabinetAI/Data` (**not** `AssistantCabinetAI-test/Data`). Her index (copy read, 91,398,144 bytes) holds 1,249 documents and 15 workbooks and **none of the five foreign files** (no `/data/...` or `/documents/...` key): the restore after step 8B put it back. The five files are to analyse until she presses Analyser |
| N-1 | Settled by that measure: the two letters of the Data folder have no row in her index, so the first document pass reads them (and embeds them: with the AI server unreachable it reports them failed, as measured in 8B). The human test of step 9 needs the AI server up for the letters |

## Taken while building the retirement of the old folder cards (explorer step 10, 21 October 2026), to confirm

The Settings panel now draws the sidebar's family sections (`FolderSettings`, `docs/CLIENT.md`, "The Settings panel's folder
sections"), and the two old cards are deleted. Decided while building, for the owner to confirm:

- **One implementation.** `FolderSettings` uses `ExplorerFamilySection` and `ExplorerFamilySetup` in their non-selectable mode, the
  same two inventories (`useFamilyInventories`, now also used by `FileExplorer`), the same folder commands
  (`useExplorerFolders`), the same two passes. Nothing was copied from the cards.
- **« Voir » on every row, no « Ouvrir ».** The panel is a place for settings: a file is opened from the sidebar. Opening from the
  panel would have to close it first and open a tab behind it; adding it later is one prop (`onOpenFile`).
- **Twelve rows with the list's own scroll bar, not "in full"** (`SETTINGS_LIST_ROWS`, `components/FolderSettings.tsx`). The handoff allowed the whole list; a folder of 1,249 documents
  drawn in full would make the panel endless, and the windowed list already costs a few dozen rows whatever the folder holds.
  `SETTINGS_LIST_ROWS` is the one number to change.
- **No fold button in the panel.** The fold state is a setting shared with the sidebar (`documentsFolded`, `tablesFolded`); the
  panel's lists are always open and never touch it.
- **« Voir le rapport » closes the panel.** The report lives in the centre, so the link closes the panel and opens the Overview.
- **The sidebar follows a clear made in the panel** through a counter held by `App` (`onFolderCleared`), added to the box's refresh
  token. A pass needs nothing: both places share the `IndexingState`.
- **What went with the cards.** `WorkFolderCard`, `DataFolderCard`, `FinderControls`, `AnalysisProgress`, `FileList`, `SelectAll`,
  `StatusDot`, `isSelectable`, `WarningGlyph`, `documentPassLines`, `dataPassLines`, `lib/unreadFormats.ts`, and what only they
  used: `analysisFraction`, `vectorPercent`, `analysisPending`, `analysisPendingDocuments`, `toggleAll`, `shownFiles`,
  `resultsToTick`, `foundAnalysed`, `foundNotAnalysed`, the finder's `showAll` / `setShowAll` / `reset`, 67 catalogue keys per
  language (`workFolder.*`, `dataFolder.*`, `finder.*`, `chat.scope*` of the cards), and their CSS (`.card*`, `.path`,
  `.work-folder__*`, `.progress*`, `.inventory*`, `.finder*`, `.status-dot*`, `.scope-picker*`, `.warning-glyph*`). `card__glyph`
  became `file-glyph` (the viewer tabs still draw it). Kept: `AnalyseButton` and `KnowledgeLink` (`FolderCardParts.tsx`),
  `CAPABILITY_KEYS`, `countPending` and `countPendingDocuments` (the notes check uses them).
- **Two user-visible sentences changed.** The knowledge dialog's reset intro said « les deux boutons Reset des cartes de dossier »;
  it now names « Effacer l'analyse » in each folder's menu (both languages). The comments of the code that spoke of "the folder
  card" were reworded (TypeScript and four Rust files, comments only).

**Not changed, as ordered.** The box, the Overview and the pipelines are untouched in behaviour. The knowledge-base sheets still
show a file key as it is (N-8) and the « documents not needed » line under a table-side refusal (N-6) is still there: neither is
part of this step.

  it lists the workbooks of the Documents folder, tagged. The script said « vide »: that was wrong (the explorer's model, step 8).
